Dates, numerals and names are given exactly as Pass 1 printed them; every `[OCR?]`/`[illegible]` flag is kept on its word. Where the extract cross-refers to an archive row, the id is given in bold; the row's own material is in that row's subsection.*

### The survey's own method, documents and questionnaire

- **The questionnaire** the book applies to each site (pp. 29–31): who the person was, when and where from; date of death and chronogram; khalifas and murids; genealogy (p. 29). The number of sajjada nashins and who is living; the caste ("قوم"); descendants and where they live; whether the site is entered in the sarkar's records (p. 30). For "مقبرہ یا شیوالہ [OCR?] یا گوردوارہ [OCR?] یا ٹھاکردوارہ [OCR?]": the maafi or jagir in land, village or cash, its amount "بوقت سلطنت جغتائی [OCR?]" and now, who granted it and whether it is for life or "نسلاً بعد نسل" (p. 30). Yearly income and expense and how they are divided; when the urs is held, its customs and distributions, and whether "ہجومِ عام" attends; silsila; graves in the compound; who built it and in what year; the building's past and present extent, with walls, dalans and enclosure (p. 30). Dimensions and heirs (p. 31). For gurdwaras, the history of the "گوروؤں [OCR?] سکھوں [OCR?]", then that of the rulers and of "حکومت انگریز [OCR?]" (p. 31).
- **Documents the ToC says are copied into the book** (ToC lines, quoted as printed at the page the ToC line stands on): "۵۹۵[OCR?] | نقل فرمان عالمگیری", a copy of an Alamgiri farman (p. 5); "۷۷۸[OCR?] | نقل ہبہ نامہ[OCR?] [illegible] شاہ فقیر[OCR?]", a copy of a deed of gift (p. 7).
- **Custodians and customs listed in the ToC**: "۸۶۲[OCR?] | حال مجاوراں مقبرہ جہانگیر", the mujawirs of Jahangir's tomb (p. 7); "۶۱۴[OCR?] | بیان رسوم فقیر حالیہ[OCR?]", the customs of the faqirs (p. 5); "۶۸۲[OCR?] | احوال گورکن[OCR?] کا", the gravediggers (p. 6).
- **Building descriptions ("تشریح عمارت") listed in the ToC**, among them "تشریح عمارت سمادہ گورو ارجن" (p. 4), "تشریح عمارت مزار جان محمد حضوری[OCR?]" (p. 5) and "تفصیل عمارت مزار عبد القادر[OCR?] ثانی[OCR?]" (p. 5).
- **Sama' sections listed in the ToC**: "۴۶۱[OCR?] | در بیان سماع", "۴۶۳[OCR?] | [illegible] اباحت سماع" (on the permissibility of sama') and "۴۷۱[OCR?] | در بیان آداب سماع" (p. 3).
- **Documents reported or copied in the body**: an Alamgiri farman for Sayyid Mahmud's khanqah (pp. 600–601; see the waqf/jagir subsection); a Persian sanad for Sailani Shah dated "بہ ماہ ماکس[OCR?] سمت ۱۱۱۲[OCR?]" (p. 534); Muhammad Shah's farman for Hamid Qari's khanqah "پاس فقیر[OCR?] کے مامون[OCR?] صاحبان کی موجود", held by the author's maternal uncles (p. 407); "فرمان و [OCR?] احکام" and a sanad of "خواجہ لواب [OCR?] فارسی [OCR?] مرحوم" and a "تولیت نامہ" for Hazrat Ishan's tomb (p. 382); a "کرسی نامہ قدمیہ [OCR?] یعنی سجرہ [OCR?]" and a sealed "محضر [OCR?]" held by the Bibi Pak Daman mujawars (p. 364); "ایک دو فرمان بادشاہی" shown by the qazis' descendants against Ahmad Din (p. 456); "تحریر مکتوبہ روضہ معلی" attesting the Qadam Rasul footprint's descent (p. 333); the Wazir Khan waqf deed, quoted (pp. 736–737); "قالیچ[OCR?] جہانگیری" cited for the Eidgah endowment (p. 709).
- **Sources and informants named in this material**: the gravediggers — Malik Ali's first account "بالا ... گورکن کے دریافت ہوا تھا", then "اب کتاب تحقیقات چشتی[OCR?] سے حال اسکا اور[OCR?] معلوم ہوا" (p. 194); "کتاب یادگار[OCR?] چشتی[OCR?]" for Sakhi Sarwar's shrine income, under "بر دستور[OCR?] اہل اسلام ملک پنجاب حصہ [illegible]" (p. 266), and a book "دگار[OCR?] حشتی[OCR?] ... مطبوعہ لاہو[OCR?]" for the Lahore Sakhi Sarwar mela (p. 256); "تحفۃ الفقرا" for the Jalali seal (p. 622); "احیاء العلوم" on the Quran and sama (pp. 477–478); Dara Shikoh's "سفینۃ الاولیاء" for attendance at Shah Madar's urs (p. 538); "زبانی مجاورین" for Jahangir's tomb (p. 863); "معمر[OCR?] اشخاص[OCR?]" at Amir Khan's grave (p. 602).
- **The author on site**: "جب بندہ بر سر موقعہ آیا", the goldsmiths' plaque shown to him (p. 462); at Hamid Qari's khanqah the author ("کمترین … مورخ[OCR?]") summoned both parties (p. 407); his finding at Sayyid Mahmud's khanqah, "نتیجہ تحقیقات یہ ہے کہ اصل مالک وارث اس مکان کی اولاد میں محمد منصور" (p. 601); his visit to the Tank of Sayyid, "نہ تو کوئی نشان مدرسہ باقی ہے" (p. 606); the numberdar of Mian Mir named "at the time of the inquiry and writing of this book" (p. 320); the fair calendar reset "three years before the inquiry" (p. 89).
- **Grave register around Mian Mir**: columns record material and condition — "پختہ", "خستہ [OCR?]", "خستہ پختہ بے چونہ [OCR?]", "خشک پختہ و چونہ [OCR?]" (pp. 324–325).

### The author, his family and his own position

- **Chishti family patronage** (chunk 001): Maulvi Baha[OCR?]-ul-Haq received a "جاگیر" from the court of "محمد شاہ[OCR?]", and the family's holding is "اب تک آباد … وغیرہ موضعات میں موجود" (p. 14). Maulvi Zia[OCR?]-ul-Haq was appointed "اتالیق[OCR?]" by the Nazim of Lahore (p. 14). A "روضہ عالیہ" began to be built for Maulvi Nizam-ud-Din after his death (p. 14). Maulvi Zia[OCR?]-ul-Haq's "عرس[OCR?]" is on "ہفتم محرم الحرام" (p. 15).
- **The family mosque and the maulvi's mazar** (pp. 18–19): the maulvi's mazar had become [illegible] the mosque; a mother [illegible] had it rebuilt; a well "بطور چندہ جمع کر کے" (by subscription) was dug, the mosque repaired, and "ملا رحمت[OCR?]" made imam. The "مہاراج" granted "موضع لبانوالہ[OCR?]", "تلوارہ[OCR?]" and land "بمقام موضع چک پورا[OCR?]", among others (p. 22), fixed "کل [illegible] روپیہ سالیانہ", with "مال النذر [OCR?]" made "معاف" (p. 23); under British rule "معافیات انکی [illegible] نقدی سرکار سے واگذار [OCR?]" (p. 26).
- **Ghulam Husain**: daily had "آٹے کی روٹیاں" baked and fed dogs "بدست خاص خود" (p. 24); had a dog's broken leg treated and paid "دو روپیہ اجرت" (pp. 24–25). His mazar is a "زیارت گاہ و خلق اللہ" with "ہر سال عرس مبارک حضرت کا"; a fuller account is promised separately (p. 26).
- **The author's tabarruk at the Basant fair**: from the cowries in his lap he counted "تین آنہ کی کوڑیاں[OCR?]" and "پونہ آنہ یعنی تین[OCR?] پیسے[OCR?] ڈبل[OCR?]"; he means to buy a "چوآنی", keep it at home, and hang it on the neck of the son he prays for (p. 78).
- **Property held by the author**: the Shah Jhangi[OCR?] site by gift with "بند[OCR?] و قبالہ" (p. 83); a registered hiba of a khanqah site to the author (pp. 778, 780–782), with a reburial "بباعث تکمیل قبضہ خود" (p. 781) and "بہت سا روپیہ خرچ ہوگیا" (p. 779).
- **The mosque of the author's wife**: "تعمیر یہہ سن بارہ سو چوہتر ہجری سی" (p. 778); two chronograms carved in marble on its front (p. 779).
- **The author's father and grandfather at Masjid Chiniyan**: "والدم [OCR?] مولوی [OCR?] محمد [OCR?] صاحب" paid for repairs of floor and fabric with "صدہا روپیہ اپنی گرہ اور زر چندہ" (p. 759) and built a long sabil with a spout; the author's maternal grandfather built a well with steps (p. 761); the imam sold the old door site (p. 759).
- **The author's maternal uncles** hold Muhammad Shah's farman for Hamid Qari's khanqah (p. 407).
- **The author's pir's rules for sama**, "as the author's pir often said" (pp. 469–470): see the sama subsection.
- **The author publishes a guarded ta'wiz**: at Mian Wadda, Ahmad Din keeps the ta'wiz hidden but gave it and its ijazat (passed "سینہ بسینہ" in Jan Muhammad's family) to the author, who publishes it; daily a chilla after "ایک ہزار درود شریف" offered to the Prophet, then writing the ta'wiz; drawn as an image with a numbered square (pp. 427–428).
- **The author as arbiter/advocate**: at Hamid Qari's khanqah he summoned both parties (p. 407); he proposes that the Muslims of Lahore petition for the release of the Taksali-gate mosque, believing "سرکار رعایا پرور" would grant it (pp. 754–755); he judges the locked door at the tomb of chunk 079 improper because "صاحبان عالیشان وغیرہ رئیسان ورعایا" come to visit, and is sure the offerings quarrel will reach the court (p. 865).

### Custodianship: sajjada nashini, gaddi, mujawirs and khadims

- **Madho Lal Husain / Hussain khanqah** (**madho-lal-hussain**): Husain prophesied a faqir "بسجادہ[OCR?] نشین" (p. 61); Madho sat as sajjada nashin at the mazar after thirteen years (p. 71). Custodianship passes by appointment and dastar, with a guardian (sarparast / "نائب سجادگی") named during a minority (p. 85); the head of the faqirs, the sargiroh, receives "حق سرگروہی" (p. 90). Gaddi and sajjada nashin seat at the khanqah (p. 97); a series of sajjada nashins buried in the enclosure (p. 98). The sajjada nashin keeps a manuscript بیاض (p. 46). The fairs, bhandaras, costs, lands, sales and debt are detailed under the row (pp. 89–90).
- **At Multan**, the khanqah's "پیر سجادہ نشین مزار حضرت مخدوم" (p. 68).
- **Mauj Darya**: three langars with hereditary servants (pp. 102–103); the gaddi nashin had wells dug (p. 106). Mauj Darya Bukhari has no sajjada nashin; a dalan and two kothris are the mutawallis' sitting place (p. 107). **Adoption and dispute** (pp. 397–398): "[illegible] شاہ" had five sons, most died "لا ولد"; "سید رحمت شاہ" was taken as "متبنی [OCR?]", "سید رحمت شاہ مدعوی [OCR?] سجادہ نشین ہو بیٹہا", and the sajjada nashini was entered "بنام سید رحمت شاہ"; he claimed "میں اولاد سید [OCR?] جلال الدین حیدر [OCR?] و آل سید شہاب الدین … سی ہوں" (p. 397). His son "کرم علی شا[ہ] [OCR?]" was sajjada nashin; now "مدد علی شاہ اور محمد شاہ" sit, recorded by "سرکار بنام سجادہ نشینان موضع مزنگ [OCR?] ضلع وتحصیل لاہور" (pp. 397–398). The author contrasts these men, "جو سجادہ نشین بن بیٹہی ہین", with "خاص سیدان بخاری کی اولا[د]" of Mauj Darya, now "سید اصغر علی شاہ و مراد علی شاہ و [illegible] علی شاہ و محبوبی [OCR?] شاہ و مسمی حسین شاہ لاہور مین" (p. 398).
- **Shah Chiragh**: sajjada nashin of the saint's offspring, "ثنا [OCR?] شاہ"; a faqir "موصی [OCR?] شاہ" holds the urs and takes the income as khadim (p. 110).
- **Abdullah Shah Qadri**: the nazr, fatiha and income are taken by "بلوچ برادران سردار خان", and "سردار خان [illegible] فتح علی [illegible]" is now owner of the place; a faqir lives in a room there (p. 111).
- **Abd al-Jalil**: a faqir "حاجی شاہ[OCR?]" now sits there (p. 119).
- **Shah Abul Maali**: on the northern platform "نوبت بہ نوبت صاحبزادہ حضرت کے بیٹھتی ہین" (p. 125); "بعہد سکھاں … سجادہ والے پھر دوبارہ اسکو تعمیر کرایا" (p. 124). Heirs and shares are under the income subsection (pp. 131–132).
- **Unnamed shrine of chunk 011**: each of the three heirs has appointed a faqir as mujawir, e.g. "قادری[OCR?] شاہ مقررہ سید امام شاہ"; these faqirs have no connection with the family's offspring (p. 134).
- **Abdul Razzaq (Shah Chiragh) tomb and mosque**: the mosque's imam is "ملا نور احمد[OCR?]", "دست نشاینده[OCR?] مولوی نگبہ[OCR?] والہ صاحب"; on his behalf "کریم بخش[OCR?]" stays as "خادم مسجد" (p. 155). Mian Rahim Bakhsh, manager of the merchants' subscription, meets all the mosque's expenses and the imams' arrangement (p. 157).
- **Haji Muhammad Saeed — ownership**: Muhammad Murad's descendants in Lahore, "محمد بخش حکاک", Ghulam Muhammad "جوسب[OCR?] فروش" and Ilah Din of "قلعہ گوجر[OCR?] سنگ[OCR?]": "یہی لوگ مالک اسمکان کے میں" (p. 159); later "منشی احمد بخش ... کام حکاکی کا کرتا ہی اور اب یہ ہی مالک اس علاوہ[OCR?] کا ہی" (p. 160).
- **Data Darbar** (**data-darbar**): the 22[OCR?] mujawirs, "۲۲[OCR?] آدمی مجاور علی الدوام حاضر رہتے ہیں" (p. 172), descend from the convert Shaikh Mahdi, "اوسکی اولاد حال جا دم و مجاور ہیں" (p. 172). Genealogy from Shaikh Mahdi through Shaikh Lutfi and a series of "…اللہ" names to Shaikh Latif Allah, whose sons were Shaikh Sulaiman and Usman (p. 174); both branches traced to living members ("موجود ہیں") — Shaikh Muhammad-ud-Din, Shaikh Ahmad Din, Sadr-ud-Din, Amir Din, Ghulam Nabi, Ghulam Muhammad, Shaikh Abid, Fateh Din and Karim Bakhsh (pp. 174–175); "ذکر اولا شیخ سلیمان … کا ختم ہوا" (p. 175). Seating for the mujawirs ("مجاوروں[OCR?]") by the kitchen (pp. 165–166). Data Ganj Bakhsh's mujawirs also hold Pir Ghaib and take its income (p. 281). (The Bakhshi Kanhiya Lal garden note says "custodianship and the mujawars are under data-darbar above", p. 345.)
- **Shah Muhammad Ghaus**: a mutawalli has been appointed (p. 178); the outer enclosure was rebuilt by the heir Mian Ghulam Nabi[OCR?] (p. 177).
- **Shaikh Tahir's khanqah**: the dalan is the "جائے[OCR?] نشست گدی نشین یعنی سجادہ نشین جناب سید شہ[OCR?] بخش[OCR?] رسول[OCR?]", with "یہاں بموسم گرما سجادہ نشین ٹھہرتا[OCR?] ہے" (p. 189). **Succession** (p. 215): Zand Ali[OCR?] of Mauza Mozang, of "وٹالہ[OCR?] والی صاحب", obtained the sajjada-nashini "زر نقد [illegible]"; his son Chiragh Shah was made sajjada nashin "سنہ[OCR?] ۱۲۶۱[OCR?] ء[OCR?]" and graced the khanqah for "دو[OCR?] [illegible] برس"; Zand Ali left in "سنہ[OCR?] ۱۲[illegible]"; Shahsawar[OCR?] bin Zand Ali, Chiragh Shah's full brother, went to Watala[OCR?] "بطمع سجادہ نشینی", became murid of the hazrats of Watala and was appointed with their permission; Chiragh Shah's son Bahadur Shah[OCR?] claims "حسب ورثہ اپنے باپ" but Shahsawar "اوسکو کچھ نہیں دیتا" (p. 215). Later an "اجازت[OCR?] نامہ میاں صاحب وٹالہ[OCR?]" and "ضبط"; Mian Pir Shah[OCR?] gaddi nashin came "بالجبر[OCR?]"; formerly "صرف گورکن لوگ عرس کرتے تھے"; income now spent on "مرمت ... وعرس ولفقہ[OCR?] سجادہ نشیں" (p. 216).
- **Office of Qazi of Lahore**: held by Qazi Ghulam Muhammad, Chiragh Shah's ancestor (p. 206).
- **Qutb Shah's takia**: on leaving for Hajj he gave the house to "منشی سردار صا[OCR?] خلف پیر محمد شہ گیلانی" (pp. 239–240); "سائیں محبوب شاہ" is made sajjada nishin in a heavily damaged passage (p. 240).
- **Sakhi Sarwar's khanqah**: the people brought from Kotli serve as "جاروب کش خانقاہ" (p. 265); Shaikh Garha holds the musalla, aftaba and nishan (p. 266); the descendants are called "میاں[OCR?]" and "مجاور[OCR?] تا حال وہی[OCR?] ہیں" (p. 266).
- **Sadr Diwan's sajjada nashins**: "میاں سی[OCR?] شاہ | میاں امین شاہ | شاہ متقی | سید امیر شاہ | میاں قطب[OCR?] شاہ", the rest "نام معلوم نہیں" (p. 268); "میاں کرم شاہ موجودہ[OCR?] سجادہ نشین ہے[OCR?]", in a clause also containing "بعہد[OCR?] مہاراجہ[OCR?] [illegible] سنگھ[OCR?] سرکار[OCR?]" (p. 268). Possession of Shah Hussain Zanjani's mazar lies with Karam Ali Shah, gaddi nashin of Sadr Diwan Sahib (p. 705).
- **Goldsmiths' pir-khana**: the faqir is "عمر شاہ فقیر بیوقوف سا", who cannot give its history (p. 279).
- **Pir Ghaib**: "جنب[OCR?] مسجد[OCR?] میاں علاوالدین علی محمد اہر[OCR?] قابض ہیں" (p. 281); "ہمیشہ ایک دو عورتیں مجاورہ یہاں بیٹھی رہتی ہیں" (p. 282).
- **Takia Sikandar Shah**: "پیر محمد شاہ گیلانی صاحب" is mukhtar and appoints the sitter (p. 283).
- **Mian Mir** (**shrine-of-mian-mir**): the appointment by Dara Shikoh's farman and the "جاگیر متعلقہ مقبرہ" are under the row (pp. 298–299); the sajjada nashins' graves are inside the enclosure (p. 299); dalans for the present sajjada nashin Mahbub Shah, "عرصہ چودہ برس سی" (p. 300). **Custodian family and succession** (pp. 317–320): after Muhammad Sharif[OCR?], his two sons "مہدی شاہ[OCR?] و شیخ نور عالی[OCR?]" held income and expenses "لنصفا لنصف" (p. 317); "حنیف شاہ[OCR?] بن معصوم شاہ[OCR?]" was appointed "مہردار[OCR?]" of the new village (p. 317). After Muhammad Shah's death, Mahbub Shah, still a child ("بجہ[OCR?]"), was appointed, with Amir Shah and then Akram/Karam Shah as guardians (pp. 318–319); in "سمت ۱۳۰۴[OCR?]" Mahbub Shah received the turban at the urs; the "عرس حضرت میا میر" was the occasion for the public turban ceremony, date not stated (p. 319). Mahbub Shah pays "خرچہ عرس" (p. 319).
- **Khair Gadah**: Shaikh Nur Muhammad's descendants hold it ("قابض", "متصرف") (p. 373).
- **Hazrat Ishan's tomb — mutawallis** (p. 382): the Nawab, seeing someone's "دیانت و پارسائی", made him mutawalli (name damaged: "مح [illegible]"); then "حافظ رحمت اللہ"; then "میاں محمد بخش صاحب"; then a daughter's line and "ماموں [OCR?]" (damaged); "میاں حافظ بخش [OCR?]" carried out repairs and put in "طاق و تختہ"; after him "فضل الدین [OCR?] محی الدین صاحب" also repaired and built a kotha.
- **Hamid Qari's khanqah — sajjada-nashini dispute** (pp. 407–411): in "سن بارہ سو [illegible]" Sharaf-ud-Din claimed the sajjada-nashini; the case went before the "[illegible] صاحب بہادر"; Fazl-ud-Din and Khair Din obtained a ruling that the sajjada nashins and owners are Muhammad Bakhsh's descendants (p. 407). The author found "مسمی رجبا[OCR?] درویش" living there for seven years by order of Ahmad Din and summoned both parties (p. 407). Fazl Din's side: Muhammad Bakhsh had appointed Sharaf-ud-Din mutawalli out of friendship; Ahmad Din: Muhammad Bakhsh had only got his permission for a grave and "اب باحق مالک بن بیٹھی ہیں" (p. 407). Ahmad Din posed three questions — deeds of descent, a "تولیت نامہ", and a shajra (p. 408); his account of the post-Hamid line is under Other saints (p. 409). Muhammad Bakhsh's family put faqirs there, "خاکی شاہ" first; when the place was becoming "ویران" they asked Sharaf-ud-Din to post a servant-darwish whose upkeep they would provide (p. 409). "مرور[OCR?] عرس خود بخود و ہاں علا[OCR?] آتا[OCR?]"; Hafiz Bakhsh later raised a claim "ایام جہلم[OCR?] میں" (p. 410). Outcome: by "دستور قدیم" Ahmad Din is "قابض و متصرف"; his khadim stays by his wish; Muhammad Bakhsh's family keep serving the present darwish and their burials are not to be obstructed; an "اقرار نامہ" was drawn up (p. 410). Signatories: Ahmad Din and his sons "محمد عطا نسیم[OCR?]", "امام الدین" and "محمد دین"; Fazl-ud-Din, Khair Din, Faiz Bakhsh, Chiragh-ud-Din, Ramzan and Amir Bakhsh (p. 411).
- **Shah Shams-ud-Din Qadiri**: a khadim "جو مکاں [illegible] لاہوری [OCR?] رہتا ہی", linked to the rauza of "حضرت شاہ اللہ اسحاق [OCR?]" in "موضع [illegible]" (p. 416).
- **Mian Wadda**: since Jan Muhammad's death the mosque has been "زیر متصرفہ سجادہ نشینان میان وڈا صاحب" (p. 425); the present imam's tenure is under Ahmad Din, whom Hafiz Darwesh made "متولی" of mosque and khanqah (p. 427). **Line and counter-claim** (pp. 450, 456): the present sajjada nashin says his ancestors have been sajjada nashins "الے الیوم", but "اشخاص معتبر" "[illegible] اسکی نہین[OCR?] کرتے", their statement promised below (p. 450); four names stand before, apparently sons — "محمد عظیم ۔ برا فرزند", "امام الدین", "محمد الدین", "غلام محمد" — whose sons not stated (p. 450). Lineage of "احمد دین سجادہ نشین حال": "احمد دین بن شرف الدین بن حافظ معزالدین بن حافظ محمود دین بن حافظ محمد صالح بن نجیر[OCR?] محمد بن حیات محمد بن شہ نواز خان" (p. 456). Counter-claim by "نتہ[OCR?] و بدر دین" and others, descendants of Sahib Khan b. Muhammad Hanif b. Qazi Muhammad Baqir b. Qazi Nur Muhammad b. Qazi Abd al-Latif b. Qazi Muhammad Ibrahim: "احمد دین سجادہ نشین حال مالک اس خانقاہ کا نہین ہے"; Qazi Muhammad Ibrahim, "قاضی لاہور", brought Mian Wadda from "موضع لنگے[OCR?]"; the khanqah site was Qazi Abd al-Latif's residence; the qazis had havelis and a jagir nearby, and "چاہ قاضیان والہ" is still theirs; they showed "ایک دو فرمان بادشاہی" (p. 456). "الی الیوم سجادہ نشیں[OCR?] ... ہمیشہ مرفہ الحال ہیں", which the book calls a karamat of Mian Wadda (p. 455).
- **Ghore Shah's khanqah**: "مہتاب شاہ فقیر سجادہ نشین اور مالک" (p. 433). Mahtab Shah also takes the Khoi Miran khui's income (p. 403).
- **Bibi Pak Daman — sajjada nashin mujawars** (p. 363): "عظیم شاہ" and "الہ دین", "پسران عبد الرحیم [OCR?]"; "احمد بخش [OCR?] پسر کرم بخش"; "محمد بخش پسر کرم بخش"; "کرم بخش خواہر زادہ عبد الرحیم مجاور"; the share of one who died "لا وارث فوت ہوا" is taken by Allah Din and Azim Shah; "یہ قدیمی [OCR?] ثبت [OCR?] سی سجادہ نشین چلی آئی [OCR?] میں" (p. 363). Mujawars' dwellings: the dalan of "کرم بخش مجاور", now held by "محمد بخش پسر کرم بخش"; a plot of "احمد بخش[OCR?] مجاور"; a house given by a "طوائف" to Karam Bakhsh; the dalan of "الہ دین[OCR?] مجاور"; a chabutra with "پانچ قبریں کلاں اور سات خورد مجاوران کی" (p. 360); an empty grave and chiraghdans made by "عظیم شاہ[OCR?] مجاور" (p. 359). Lineage: "عبد الرحیم بن شیخ امام بخش بن شیخ محمد رضا [OCR?] … بن شیخ ایوب بن شیخ ذکریا بن شیخ محمد بن شیخ عبد [OCR?] المعروف با [OCR?] با خاکی [OCR?]" (p. 364). (Pass 1 read the khanqah on pp. 353–355 as Bibi Pak Daman's; see Doubts.)
- **Goldsmiths' graveyard**: five goldsmith custodians, "سرپرست[OCR?] پانچ نفر … جنکو نام بند و نسبت میں درج": "[illegible] زرگر ــ ر لدو[OCR?] زرگر ــ اعظم[OCR?] زرگر ــ فیض بخش زرگر ــ عمر دین زرگر" (p. 462).
- **Takia Kamil Shah**: sajjada nashin "حیدر شاہ"; the chain runs back through "مالک شاہ", "بہادر شاہ" and "دیوان کامل شاہ" to several "سرمست" names, continuing on p. 531 into "حضرت شاہ مدار غازی کا شجرہ و سلسلہ مداریہ" (pp. 530–531). Eleven successors' graves "بطرف مشرق چار دیواری میں گیارہ قبریں": "جمعی شاہ[OCR?]", "گلا شاہ[OCR?]", "بہار شاہ[OCR?]", "والشا[OCR?]", "کسوی شاہ[OCR?]", "حبوں شاہ[OCR?]", "بتاب شاہ[OCR?]", "مالک شاہ[OCR?]", "شوقی شاہ[OCR?]", "بہاو شاہ[OCR?]", "اصفی شاہ[OCR?]", "اسلم کا[OCR?]" (p. 530).
- **Sailani Shah — Madari chain** (p. 532): from his sons and murids through many "شاہ" and "سرمست" names to "حضرت سر شاہ مدار", then "شاہ مسعود" → "شکر گنج[OCR?]" → "خواجہ بایزید[OCR?]" → "خواجہ حسن جی مکے[OCR?]" → "حضرت خواجہ حسن بصری" → "علی المرتضی" → the Prophet.
- **Sayyid Mahmud's khanqah**: "اس خانقاہ پیر الہی شاہ فقیر خادم انکا بطور سجادہ نشین ہے"; chain of khadims — Ilahi Shah khadim of Sayyid Hamid Shah, Hamid Shah of Sayyid Ghulam Muhyi al-Din, then Sayyid Nur, then Sayyid Abd Allah Qadiri, then Shah Muhammad Hazuri (p. 602); a faqir "مسمی آبی[OCR?] شاہ" was present (p. 595); Ilahi Shah draws a "وظیفہ" (p. 599); the ownership finding for Muhammad Mansur (p. 601).
- **Hasu Teli khanqah — dispute** (p. 610): Husain Shah calls himself a khadim of the family, but "وغیرہ لوگ اسکو اس خاندان کا خادم نہیں کہتے"; the son of "امام بخش[OCR?]" says "میرا باپ کسی کا مرید نہ تھا"; the book's "اصلی حال": Mian Khan the first mujawir; his khadim Hamrah Shah, who made his elder son khadim; that son was incapable and the place fell into neglect; "یہ حسین شاہ مالک بن بیٹھا اور روپیہ خرچ کرکے" took it over. Faqir "حسین شاہ" lives there and built the whole enclosure; before it was "صرف ایک چبوترہ" (p. 608).
- **Haji Abd al-Karim's khanqah**: sajjada nashin "نور الدین مالک مکان", with "رجب بخش[OCR?] اللہ شاہ" and "مائی مبارک بی بی دختر غلام شاہ"; graves of "عظیم شاہ خادم اعلی حضور" and "غلام شاہ سجادہ نشین" (p. 639).
- **Mastan Shah's takia**: the makandar is Dari[OCR?] Shah, served by the Ayub princes (p. 627).
- **Respect for the Gilani sadat**: "اکثر اہل اسلام بلکہ ہر باشندان[OCR?] لاہور انکا ادب دل و جان سے کرتے ہیں" (p. 524).
- **Sadr Jahan's tomb**: the descendants are "متولی"; a list of taluqas held by named descendants (pp. 648–649).
- **Shah Kamal's tomb**: care by sweeping and lamp-lighting by Nihal Shah (p. 661).
- **Sher Ali Shah's makan**: the succession contest among Rahim Shah, Shamsher Ali and Din Shah, and the refusal of the Lahore-area faqirs, is under Other saints (p. 663).
- **Halim[OCR?] Shah / Shah Badr**: "دم تحریر اسمکان پر قبضہ درزیو[OCR?] کا ہی"; "قدیم الایام سے اس مکان سے اولاد حضرت کو کچھ علاقہ نہین ہے صرف یہی درزمی[OCR?] ہر طرحسی[OCR?] مالک ہین" (p. 671).
- **Syed Ali Shah**: after his death "خادمان خانقاہ" continued to serve, and "اب تک یہ مکان آباد ہی" (p. 707).
- **Marg Naini takia**: a khadim lives in the dalans (garbled) (p. 710).
- **Abdi Shah**: the mason's sons keep the lamps (p. 773).
- **Masjid Wazir Khan**: hereditary imamate from forefathers; current imam "میان محمد ولد امام الہ بخش" (pp. 744, 747); the shops below the mosque and attached houses are still held by the descendants and khuddam (p. 747).
- **Miran Badshah mazar**: sajjada nashin "امام الدین بن حسن دین" (p. 747); a hujra occupied by "امام الدیں ولد حسن الدیں مجاور" (p. 744).
- **Jahangir's tomb**, "زبانی مجاورین": Shah Jahan gave gardening and mujawiri to "میان غلام محمد قوم آرائین", with furnishings worth "لاکہا روپیہ"; two Quran-reciting hafiz per cell; hafiz paid from the royal treasury; later a king "واحد [OCR?] شاہ" supported them; the Sikh period ruined the furnishings (text breaks off) (p. 863). Forty cells around the gallery, some held by mujawirs: "شرف دین مجاور | قطب الدین مجاور | امام الدین مجاور | نظام الدین مجاور | مہتاب [OCR?]" (pp. 856–857).
- **Tomb of chunk 079 (opening)**: Hikmatullah and Jawaya live in Shahdara and practise as hakims ("حکمت کرتی [OCR?]"); Imam Din, Qutb-ud-Din, Sharafdin and Nizam-ud-Din attend day and night and sweep (p. 865).
- **Sikh, Udasi and jogi gaddis**: see the Sikh/Hindu/jogi subsection (gaddi succession at Than Bhairo p. 564; Tahli Sahib p. 679; Shahid Ganj p. 769; Sahib Singh samadh pp. 770–771; gurdwara of chunk 046 p. 514).

### Division of income, shares and turns

- **Shah Abul Maali — heirs and shares** (pp. 131–132): "وارث حضرت کر دو قسم" — "پوترین[OCR?]" and nawasas (p. 131). Named living descendants "سید عالم شاہ" (p. 131); "سید مہر شاہ", "لا ولد ہے" (p. 132). Nawasa heirs: Sayyid Chiragh Shah and others, "ولدان سید غازی شاہ"; Sayyid Hakim Shah, Sayyid Haidar Shah, Jahangir Shah, Muhammad[OCR?] Shah; Fateh Shah and Sardar Shah, "برادران سید نوٹی[OCR?] شاہ مرحوم"; "معصوم شاہ"; "منٹو[OCR?] شاہ بن امیر شاہ"; Karam Shah; "سید ملال[OCR?] شاہ"; Ramzan Shah (p. 132). Turns: "چودہ[OCR?] روز نو اسہ[OCR?] اور دہ [illegible] روز شیرہ[OCR?] مقررہ پر حاضر رہتی ہیں", whatever comes on those days is theirs, "[illegible] گفت و گرفت نہین ہوئی" (p. 132). "مدر و چڑھاوہ" divided "خواہ کوئی غیر حاضر ہو یا حاضر" (p. 132). A third share to "سید پیر و سہا[OCR?] ور شاہ پسران سید حاکم شاہ"; the other two parts into "سات[OCR?]" shares, a seventh to the sons of Nauti[OCR?] Shah; the remaining six into "پانچ[OCR?]": one to "دختران معصوم شاہ"; one to Chiragh Shah and "[illegible] ور شاہ ساکنان لاہور"; one to Sayyid Pir Shah; one to "حسن علی[OCR?] شاہ"; one halved between "سید حاکم شاہ پسر حیدر شاہ ساکن کوٹلی [illegible] خاں" and the late Hakim Shah's line; some shares handed to "زوجہ سید پیر شاہ"; of one line "معلوم نہیں" (p. 132).
- **Turn-based custodianship, unnamed shrine of chunk 011**: each sahibzada keeps the income of his turn; the others are not barred ("ممانعت[OCR?] نہیں") from receiving offerings; an offering from a murid who names his own sahibzada goes to that pir ("اوسی کے پیر کو پہونچتا ہے") (p. 133). Oil, wick and cloth given in shares ("حصہ وار[OCR?]") at the urs (p. 133).
- **Data Darbar**: income divided among the mujawirs by head; a child born to a mujawir receives a share and the share of one who dies lapses; rules for one who goes away (p. 172). "جسقدر زن و مرد مجاوران خدم علیہی[OCR?] سوجو[OCR?] دین سب سبب برابر حصہ پاتے بین" — men and women take equal shares; the chunk breaks off on the rule for absentees (p. 175).
- **Mian Mir — division** (pp. 317–319): Mai Mehr Nishan ("مہر نشان[OCR?]") married "عیبا شاہ[OCR?]"; their four sons "نتہو شاہ[OCR?]", "اکرم شاہ[OCR?]", "محمد شاہ[OCR?]" and "اکبر شاہ[OCR?]" take a fourth of the income and pay a fourth of the expenses (p. 317). Masum Shah's daughter "بی بی زہرہ[OCR?]" married "میان قادر بخش[OCR?] بن مراد بخش[OCR?]"; Hanif Shah's four daughters: Mai Fazl-un-Nisa ("فضل النسا[OCR?]") married "خود حد بخش[OCR?] بن قادر بخش[OCR?]", lineage back to "محمد شفیع[OCR?]"; Akram-un-Nisa ("اکرم النسا[OCR?]") married Sayyid Akbar Shah of "شہر قبورہ[OCR?]", her son "احمد شاہ[OCR?]" takes no share but could; Khair-un-Nisa ("خیر النسا[OCR?]") married the imam of Masjid Wazir Khan, her two daughters' lines could also take shares (p. 317). Bibi Zahra took her share "موجب حکم شرعی"; the rest of the computation is garbled (p. 318). The sajjadagi income was divided "چار حصوں پر" "حسب الحکم و مشائی[OCR?] شریعت محمدی" (p. 318). **Current division** (p. 319): three shares held by Khuda Bakhsh's descendants, named as "متولیون[OCR?]" — "محبوب شاہ[OCR?] لپسر[OCR?] محمد شاہ[OCR?] بن خدا بخش[OCR?]", "الف شاہ[OCR?] بن کریم شاہ[OCR?] بن خدا بخش[OCR?]", "مہر شاہ[OCR?] بن قطب شاہ[OCR?] بن امیر شاہ[OCR?] بن خدا بخش[OCR?]"; they are in agreement, Mahbub Shah supports each, and anyone who separates gets one of the three shares; the fourth share is divided equally among the four sons of "عیسی شاہ[OCR?]", who also take their mother "مہر النسا[OCR?] بنت حنیف شاہ[OCR?]"'s share; Fazl-un-Nisa gave her paternal share to Khuda Bakhsh; Karam-un-Nisa's and Khair-un-Nisa's descendants take no share but may by sharia (p. 319). (As printed, Mehr Nishan's husband is "عیبا شاہ[OCR?]" on p. 317 and "عیسی شاہ[OCR?]" on p. 319; Akram-un-Nisa (p. 317) appears as Karam-un-Nisa (p. 319); "مہر نشان" p. 317 vs "مہر النسا" p. 319.)
- **Bibi Pak Daman — division** (pp. 363–364; numerals flagged): "سال بہر میں اڑتالیس [OCR?] حجرات [OCR?] آتی [OCR?] میں", split among Allah Din, Azim Shah ("سا [OCR?] ڈہی [OCR?] او نیس [OCR?]") and Muhammad Bakhsh ("نو حجرات [OCR?] تین"), figures garbled (p. 363). Each month twelve days' income to Azim Shah, twelve to Allah Din, the remaining days to Muhammad Bakhsh (p. 363). Graves: "حق کھودائی قبر" to the labourer; the rest in four parts, one to Muhammad Bakhsh and one each of the remaining three to Allah Din and Azim Shah, who also take the heirless heir's share (p. 363). Offerings ("منت [OCR?]") shared "علی قدر [OCR?] [illegible]"; the example for a rupee is garbled: "باع [OCR?] آنحق [OCR?]", "گیارہ [OCR?] وآنہ [OCR?]" (pp. 363–364). The mujawars attend daily at their places; a visitor's intentional distribution stands; "اگر صرف ایک کی کو دیوی [OCR?] تو وہ صاحب لوٹ [OCR?] لیتا ہی" (p. 364). "عرس کے روز خرچ و چرمت [OCR?] مشترک ہی"; urs date (not stated) (p. 364).
- **Gravediggers' shares at Miani**: one line of gorkans holds the work, income split "نصفاً نصف", part paid to the sajjada nashin (p. 209); gorkan hereditary shares and fee division (pp. 213–214) — detailed under the Miani bullet.
- **Abd al-Qadir Thani — before the dispute**: "ایک حصہ بخرچ لنگر", a second share to dependents, the rest "نصفا نصف" between the brothers Sayyid Haji and Sayyid Sultan Akbar; murids' offerings during the saint's life went to Sayyid Haji alone (p. 651).
- **Shah Jamal khanqah**: Thursday chadhat in cash and kind shared among the keepers (p. 692); income about 300 rupees a year; dispute between the Fakhr-ud-Din family and the sweeper-mujawirs over attendance (p. 693).
- **Shah Gada**: the land's yield split into five shares, among Kali Shah, "لڈو[OCR?] می[OCR?] شاہ", Khair Shah, Mamun Shah and one illegible name; some sharers do not live there "مگر حصہ لیتی[OCR?] ہیں" (p. 699).
- **Gurdwara of chunk 046**: "دو بھائی" are "مکان کی آمدنی میں حصہ دار" (p. 514).
- **Tomb of chunk 079 (opening) — offerings dispute** (pp. 864–865): previously all offerings were split equally every day; when the settlement (بندوبست) came the mujawirs quarrelled; "محکمہ بندوبست" appointed two arbitrators, "دیال شاہ کہتری ساہوکار ساکن شاہدرہ" and "عبد اللہ چودہری لونگہ [OCR?]" (p. 864); they split the garden into three shares; Hikmatullah then set ten-day turns (نوبت), each holder keeping up to four rupees and sharing anything above; during Hikmatullah and Jawaya's turn the door is kept locked; the others complain that offerings from veiled women or notables go inside unseen and that their living depends only on this tomb's income (p. 865).
- **Shah Rahmat Ullah Qureshi**: "گذارہ انکی اولاد کا صرف نذرانہ پیری و مریدی ہے ہی" (p. 329).

### Waqf, jagir, muafi and land

- **Mughal-period grants**: "معاف و واگذار" under "فرامین شاہان[OCR?] جغتائی[OCR?]" at Madho Lal Husain (p. 80); Mughal grants among successive states' patronage, land lost at the English settlement (pp. 88–89, 95). Mauj Darya: Akbar's jagir of "[illegible] لاکھ [OCR?] روپیہ", income spent on faqirs and the poor (p. 102); an allowance "عہد اکبر شاہ سے [illegible] روپیہ مقررہ تھا وہ اب [illegible] کوٹھی [OCR?] حساب [OCR?] مکلوڈ [OCR?] صاحب [illegible]" — what became of it cannot be read (p. 106); "معافیات [OCR?] لاہور" handed over by Mauj Darya to Safi al-Din (p. 397).
- **Shah Muhammad Ghaus**: "سرکار کی طرف سے جاگیر[OCR?] کثیر تخمیناً بیس[OCR?] ہزار [illegible]"; "سابق[OCR?] میں قیمت ایک لاکھ[OCR?] روپیہ کی تھی"; people "دبا بیٹھے[OCR?] ہیں" part of it (p. 178).
- **Shah Bilawal**: "موضع شحو پورہ[OCR?]" granted ("عطا کر دیا") in connection with "سرکار[OCR?]", grantor not legible (p. 180).
- **Unnamed shrine of chunk 011**: "کوٹ[OCR?] سیالاں علاقہ لاہور تحصیل چوبیاں[OCR?]", "موضع جان پور[OCR?] علاقہ شیخوپورہ ضلع گوجرانوالہ", and land "مہلن[OCR?] والی", remitted ("معاف و واگذار[OCR?]") by the former sarkar; Syed Imam Shah takes the Sialan village's income for the urs; Jan Pur and the land go to Syed Mahin Shah, "نصف[OCR?]", for his urs (p. 134).
- **The Sayyid's khanqah**: by British grace, per the report of "کرنیل سر منگمری[OCR?] لارنس[OCR?]", the revenue of Panj Graian is remitted and spent on "معارف خانقاہ سید مرحوم" (p. 139).
- **Shah Kalu Chishti**: a "وصیت" for monthly expenses of a majlis and lamp-lighting (p. 144).
- **Haji Muhammad Saeed**: "مقام کوٹ کمان[OCR?] علاقہ جلال آباد میں ایک گاؤں[OCR?] بنام انکو معاف ہی" (p. 159).
- **Recitation endowment at Data Darbar**: Qur'ans endowed so that reciters recite and send the merit to the Prophet; "قاری موظف" appointed at each mazar listed (pp. 167–169).
- **Shah Sharaf**: "آٹھ[OCR?] بیگھہ زمین مزروعہ و سات[OCR?] بیگھہ غیر مزروعہ انکی خانقاہ کے ساتھ معاف ہی", income to Mahtab faqir (pp. 221–222).
- **Chaubara of Jiju Bhagat**: "پانچ روپیہ یومیہ اس مکان کا تھا"; land "کنال[OCR?] سات مرلہ" "معاف تھی"; a yearly "ہتر[OCR?] روپیہ" from the "سرکار" by "مہا راجہ معافی", now "مسدود ہوگیا" (p. 252); cultivated land of "سات گھمانو[OCR?]" (p. 251); a rule that one who acts against custom forfeits his heirs' standing (p. 252, heavily flagged).
- **Goldsmiths' pir-khana**: "اٹھدہ[OCR?] کنال زمین غیر مزروعہ" (p. 279).
- **Bibi Pak Daman**: land east and south of the khanqah given to the convert's descendants (p. 358); old holdings continued "سب حاکموں", "چار جاہ[OCR?] مزروعہ" granted to the khuddam (p. 359); land "حالیس [OCR?] گھا ہو [OCR?]" is "واگدار ہی [OCR?]", with "قلعہ گوجر سنگھ" and "سرداران" in the garbled passage (p. 364).
- **Rukn al-Din takia**: "عہد ساہاں [OCR?] [? شاہاں]" an "عطا و معاش [OCR?]" attached; "اب عہد سکہاں [OCR?] سی ضبط میں" (p. 348).
- **Abu al-Khair**: "مال گزاری[OCR?]" of villages ("دیہات"; count printed "جہتر[OCR?]") linked to the hakim of Lahore and Abu al-Khair in Muhammad Shah's reign, damaged (p. 372); a jagir in Peshawar district "عطیہ شاہان سلف" and "معافیات و یومیہ" fixed in Lahore (p. 372).
- **Hazrat Ishan's tomb**: from the time of "عبد الصمد خاں صاحب مرحوم" [rupees] granted to "حافظ محمد بخش صاحب" as "خدمت گدار [OCR?]"; the grant ran until "عہد میر سو [illegible]"; when "عملہ داری منتیر [OCR?]" it was "صط [OCR?]" (p. 382).
- **The opening hazrat of chunk 035**: Jahangir's "روزینہ معقول" (p. 386); Shah Jahan's "ایک لاکہ[OCR?] ٹکہ[OCR?]", divided between the Kashmiri khanqah building, his Lahore khanqah and takia, and the needy (p. 387).
- **Hamid Qari's khanqah — grant and jagir** (p. 407): Muhammad Shah's farman; later rulers kept the "معافی"; the jagir money reached Muhammad Bakhsh's family yearly; "بعہد مہاراجہ صاحب" the jagirdar "بابو باج[OCR?] سنگھ المشہور[OCR?] بابو رای[OCR?]" kept paying; under "عملداری سرکار انگریزی" "بابو گیان سنگھ" refused and offered cultivable land with a "چاہ مسدودہ[OCR?]", entered in the settlement ("ہند[OCR?] و لسبت[OCR?]", read as bandobast); the three brothers restored the well, Gyan Singh later sold it; the sajjada nashins, "نا واقف قانون سرکار", kept silent but now say they will file a claim at settlement.
- **Ghore Shah**: land "ایک کنال [illegible] مرلہ" and a well granted by Ranjit Singh; the cultivator "شمس[OCR?]" takes "دو حصہ پیداواری[OCR?]" and gives one fixed share (p. 432).
- **The jhok of Shah Abul Maali** (p. 480): Sohni[OCR?] Shah planted (?) trees ("بیڑیاں بھی نذے[OCR?] لگا کر") and made them waqf, saying no one should seize them after him; built "ایک چاہ کلان چرخ چوب والہ" with his pir's permission; "سولہ[OCR?] کہا[OCR?] نو[OCR?] زمین" granted "وعدہ سرکار سے", "تا قیام جھوک و اگذار[OCR?]"; the faqir takes its income and gives a traveller one meal.
- **Mian Wadda**: two wells ("دو جاہاں[OCR?]") linked to "سرکار رکبت[OCR?] سگہ[OCR?]"; grants ("عطیہ") from "سلف", "عالمگیر[OCR?]" named (p. 455); "ایک چاہ موضع ٹنکے[OCR?]" and land at "لون[OCR?] میانی[OCR?]" linked to Ahmad Din (pp. 455–456).
- **Mohabbat Shah's takia**: "چار[OCR?] بیگہ[OCR?] زمین مزروعی[OCR?]" "معاف[OCR?]" in Madari Shah's lifetime, "اب ضبط سرکار ہے" (p. 491).
- **Gurdwara of chunk 046 — "معافی"** (p. 514): one mauza with an annual sum (illegible); a second mauza "ضلع سیالکوٹ[OCR?]"; "[illegible] ہزار[OCR?]" and "روپیہ" figures; "ایک چاہ[OCR?]"; "سرکاری" and "وقف[OCR?]" in damaged context.
- **Gilani grants**: Akbar's offer of a thousand bigha refused by Sufi Ali (p. 523); Akbar offered "ہزار بیگہہ[OCR?] زمین" in "علاقہ[OCR?] فیروز پور" to the ancestor from Uch (p. 525); "ایک موضع" granted by "عالم با و ساہ" (read: Alamgir Badshah?) in Sayyid Fazil's time (p. 528).
- **Sailani Shah**: land "تین گھمانو[OCR?] تین کنال دس مرلہ" (p. 531); the sons jointly cultivate "چودہ[OCR?] بیگہ[OCR?] زمین" (pp. 531–532); muaf "بعہد مہاراجہ بہادر" (garbled), attested by "خواجہ ملک دیوان احمد یار شاہ" and the extra assistant commissioner of Lahore; a "معاملہ مبلغ" fixed "بشرائط[OCR?] نمبرداران دیہہ"; they now intend to petition the sarkar (p. 532); the Persian sanad "خارج از جمع[OCR?] از موضع بابو سابو … در وجہ[OCR?] فقیر سیلانی شاہ و معاف", "بہ ماہ ماکس[OCR?] سمت ۱۱۱۲[OCR?]" (p. 534). Rahman Shah's takia: "زمین مزروعہ لوہ[OCR?] بیگہ دو کنال دس مرلہ" muaf "تاحین حیات" to the three sons, irrigated from the well (p. 533).
- **Sayyid Mahmud's khanqah**: "اس خانقاہ[OCR?] کو مبلغ[OCR?] [illegible] میں ایک موضع تھا" (p. 601). **The Alamgiri farman** (pp. 600–601), held by "سید احمد شاہ و حسن[OCR?] شاہ و حسین شاہ و محمد شاہ اولاد حضرت", grants land in "دار السلطنت لاہور" in the name of "مسمات نور خان[OCR?]" (the farman text itself reads "مسمات نور خاتون وغیرہ") (p. 600); "زوجہ حضرت جان [illegible]"; "بہ مدد و معاش"; "آمادہ لایق زراعت"; "خارج از جمع"; "بسم اللہ الرحمن الرحیم بخط عربی بحرف سیاہ" and a "طغرا بزرگ سرخ" reading "یا ایہا الناس اطیعوا اللہ"; the seal, a large round seal with smaller ones, reads "محی الدین محمد [illegible] ۱۰۸۰[OCR?] ابو المظفر" (p. 600); close "تاریخ چهاردهم شهر ربیع الاول سنه [illegible] جلوس" (p. 601). The Ranjit Singh parwana is under **samadhi-of-maharaja-ranjit-singh** (p. 600).
- **Tank of Sayyid**: jagir "سرو اسطی[OCR?] مدہ[OCR?] معائش": "موضع راوال[OCR?]" "قریب ہزار و ام[OCR?]", and "موضع صالح پور[OCR?]" (p. 605).
- **Hasu Teli**: "[OCR?]چہ بیگہ زمین مزروعہ معہ چاہ" (p. 608).
- **Village Jiyo[OCR?] and Shah Hakim**: "معافی" of wells and cultivated land; "انعامداری سرکار خالصہ ضبط ہو گئی"; "بعہد ملکہ معظمہ [OCR?] اٹھارہ سو [illegible]", Ali Pur was "جاگیر راجہ و میاں [illegible]" (p. 613).
- **Mastan Shah's takia**: "چودہ بیگہ زمین معافی مقبرہ احمد علی شاہ صاحب کے ساتھہ واگذار ہے" (p. 627).
- **Hidayat Ali Khan family**: a village held in muafi, linked to "قصہ نور الدین", "وہ بھی اوکی قصہ سے ذیل[OCR?] گیا" (p. 630).
- **Haji Abd al-Karim's khanqah**: a well with land "سرکار سے تا قیام خانقاہ معاف" (p. 639).
- **Abd al-Qadir Thani**: "جب ملکہ[OCR?] پیران ہوا اور عہد خان بہادر بھی بگڑ گیا تو وہ جاگیر[OCR?] بھی ابار[OCR?] ہو گئی" (p. 651).
- **Jhangar Shah's makan**: one rupee a day "بیطور وہرم[OCR?] ارنہہ[OCR?]" and one paisa a day per house[OCR?] or shop in Ranjit Singh's time; "اب تمیسر[OCR?] ہو دن فقط شہر ہی" (p. 657).
- **Lakhpat Rai's tank**: "آٹھ[OCR?] بیگھہ", of which "پانچ[OCR?] بیگھہ[OCR?]" south and "تین بیگھہ" north (pp. 669–670).
- **Halim[OCR?] Shah / Shah Badr**: "تاحال بارہ بیگہ زمین متعلقہ اس خانقاہ کے سرکار سے معافت ہے" (p. 671).
- **Tahli Sahib**: under Ranjit Singh a daily rupee[OCR?] and a well with land revenue-free; that land has gone to the railway, leaving "صرف اٹہارہ کنال" (p. 679); later grants (damaged) "موضع [OCR?] [illegible] ایک من", "موضع کہووال[OCR?]" (p. 682). Pir Makki's revenue-free land is under that row (p. 675).
- **Fata Shah[OCR?] khanqah**: "زمین مزروعہ و غیر مزروعہ", "معافی[OCR?]" since earlier times (damaged) (p. 697). **Shah Gada**: land "قدیم سے معاف" (p. 699).
- **Jangli Chiragh Shah**: "پانچ گاہ[OCR?] اور [illegible] مرلہ[OCR?] زمین", with "سرکار" (damaged) (p. 706).
- **Eidgah endowment**, citing "قالیچ[OCR?] جہانگیری" (p. 709): Jahangir sanctioned "مبیں[OCR?] لاکھہ روپیہ"; under "خواجہ ایاز[OCR?]" a mosque was built with a "ترپولیہ[OCR?]" bazaar — three bazaars, east, north and south, each "دو سو ستر دوکانیں معہ بالاخانہ", endowed (wording damaged) for expenses; monthly rent income "اڑھائی ہزار روپے[OCR?]"; on "نورجہان بیگم"'s recommendation "سید مقبول علی[OCR?]" was appointed mutawalli, "مولوی عنایت جمیں[OCR?]" mudarris, "حافظ حبیب" and an imam, plus "پنجاہ[OCR?] کس[OCR?]" other servants.
- **Begumpura**: "راجہ سنا[OCR?] چند" gave it to his Brahmins with the "معاملہ" fixed at "ایک سو رپیہ سالانہ"; later the Brahmins got a jagir in the Kohistan instead (damaged) (p. 721).
- **Mehr Manga's family**: ancestral and self-acquired land, "قریب چار صد بیکے[OCR?]" in Baghbanpura, shared half-and-half between Azimullah's and Hafiz Muhammad's descendants (p. 725); shares in named wells ("چاہ بارہ دری والی", others damaged) and garden land in "نگہ[OCR?]"/"بیگہ[OCR?]", including "ایک سو چار بیگہ[OCR?]" (pp. 725–726).
- **Waqf doctrine against practice**: the book quotes "الوقف لا یملک[OCR?]" and says a mosque is waqf, yet this mosque is held privately (damaged) (p. 728).
- **Waqf of the Wazir Khan mosque** (pp. 736–737): endowed shops on both sides of the road with upper rooms, a large sarai, hammam and other properties in Lahore; "لا تباع و لا توہب ولا یرہن"; management to the founder for life, then his son "محمد سعید جان[OCR?]" and descendants; final beneficiaries "الفقرا المسلمین"; imam/khatib, muezzin and madrasa teachers paid from the rents (figures damaged: "بہ نارہ[OCR?] روپیہ", "جہار[OCR?]"); shops outside the eastern gate rent-free for "صحافاں[OCR?] کتب اسلامیہ"; family shares of rent; "کما ہو الحکم فی المذہب الحنفی". Further (p. 747): "واسطے[OCR?] دوامی استحکام و رونق مسجد" a two-sided bazaar from the mosque to "بدلی[OCR?] دروازہ", a sarai and hammam, katras, havelis, and one or two villages; villages and many houses later passed out of the family's hands and many old khuddam are now in poor state (heavily damaged) (p. 748); the north katra/tavila "نواب وزیر خان نے وقف کرکے شامل مسجد ہذا" (p. 741).
- **Shahid Ganj (Mani Singh) grants under the Sikhs** (p. 768): "گیارہ سو روپیہ کی جاگیر" in "علاقہ ترن تارن ضلع امرتسر"; revenue-free wells at "موضع چوکاک [OCR?] وال و مظفرپور [OCR?]", "شرقپور [OCR?] ضلع لاہور" (a well of fifty rupees), near "پل مالی [OCR?]"; a table: "موضع سکیاں [OCR?]" chah "٥ [OCR?]", "علاقہ نجاس [OCR?]" one well, "بیلہ [OCR?] بہای دستی [OCR?] رام" one well. Under British rule a jagir released to Jiwan[OCR?] Singh for life, to continue to his heirs, with one-fourth of income paid as nazrana; his death "سمت ١٩١ [OCR?] بکرماجیتی" (p. 769, damaged). Dues from Lahore grain markets in Sikh times: one chhatank per bullock-load, half a ser per cow[OCR?]-load, half a pao per camel-load, and two pai from every traveller; travellers still give voluntarily (p. 769).
- **Dori Shah's khanqah**: "سو بگیہہ زمین مزروعہ" released by the nazim of Lahore "تا قیام خانقاہ"; under Sikh rule ("حسب رواج عملداری سکہان") the land was taken over (p. 776).
- **Dara-wife mosque**: a bazaar endowed to it; sold for "چہہ ہزار روپیہ" to the railway (p. 772).
- **Mai Bhagi's takia**: "تین[OCR?] بگہہ مزروعہ" "واگذار" from the sarkar (p. 791). **Chandrat**: "ساٹہہ[OCR?] بیگہ" attached, "تیس[OCR?] بیگہہ بصیغہ انعام بنام مقام", the rest revenue-free for Booti Nath's lifetime (p. 792). Jogi gaddi income is "زر معافی" (p. 795).
- **Shalamar**: "شرح زمین محدودہ شہلا باغ", bigha figures for each garden, all [illegible] (p. 788).
- **Jahangir's tomb**: a jagir of "موضع منگل [OCR?] و [illegible]" and revenue-free land of the wells (p. 863).
- **Granthis' dera and Jawahar Singh's samadhi**: see the Sikh subsection (pp. 866–871).
- **Somnath** (as described): two thousand villages "معاف" for its expenses (p. 805).

### Offerings, vows, healing customs and income

- **Cash offerings to saints**: the Khan-e Khanan gave "پانسو[OCR?] روپیہ" to Husain (p. 67) and "دو سو روپیہ نذرانہ" at the Zakariya khanqah, returned (p. 68); a Mughal places "مبلغ پچیس[OCR?] روپیہ" before Mian Mir, who gives it at once to a poor Mughal (p. 289); Dara's purse ("ہمیانی") ended in "بادشاہ کے بیت المال" (p. 291).
- **Food offering**: villagers brought Husain "نان مرغن با شیر و شکر", after which his companions danced (p. 62).
- **Hussain khanqah**: on the urs day nazr at the takht; ghilaf on Madho's grave "بروز عرس و سیر"; a lamp lit daily in the chiragdan (p. 99).
- **Votive clay horses at Ghore Shah**: "اب بھی وہان لوگ مٹی سے گھوڑے بطور نذر چڑھاتی ہین", horses on the wall and grave platform (p. 123); a heap of horses ("ڈھیر [illegible] گھوڑوں کا") west of the platform (p. 433); "گلی گھوڑی طلائی اور نقرئی اور اصلی لوگ وہان چڑھاتے ہین" (p. 435).
- **Vows at Pir Zumurrdi[OCR?]**: "جو کوئی آکر منت مانے ہے کہ اگر فلاں کام میرا ہو جاوے تو میں ... کی مدر دوں[OCR?]"; sweet rotis ("شیریں روتیاں[OCR?]"); women come ("اکہر[OCR?] وں[OCR?] یہاں آتی ہیں"); "عرس ان حضرت کو" in a broken line (p. 198).
- **Payment to mujawirs**: when Muhammad Sultan comes, "ایک رویہ مجاوران کو دیجاتا ہے" (p. 199).
- **Pir Ghaib — healing and income**: for a throat ailment ("گلے پر درد ورم[OCR?] یا سوج") people take up "[OCR?] پیالہ[OCR?] مٹی کا" and pass the clay over the place; when cured they bring "مصری", "شکر" and "زر نقد[OCR?]"; "ہمیشہ غلاف وغیرہ پارچات بطور نذر آتی ہیں"; "بہت آدمی واسطے زیارت کے آتے ہیں اور بہت آمدنی ہوتی ہے" (p. 281); "تمام لوگ اس خانقاہ کو بزرگ کرکے مانتے ہیں" (p. 282).
- **Dargahi Shah**: "آمدنی کچھ نہیں ہے"; some come for salaam; children brought to "وانیاں والہ چاہ[OCR?]" and bathed; offerings brought; bread given "چاہ و آ کے زمیندار کو" (p. 224).
- **Chaubara of Jiju Bhagat**: "زائرین اقوام مندر یہان مٹھی[OCR?] میکنی[OCR?] کو آتی میں اور نذر جبر[OCR?] ہاتی میں" (p. 247).
- **Mian Mir**: after Ranjit Singh's repentance the mujawirs became rich; "آجکل بہی معافیان"; hundreds of nobles brought offerings daily and beggars went home with goods in the evening (p. 297); at the urs "صدہا روپیہ نذر کا جمع ہوتا ہی" (p. 305); shop rent in the outer wall "سجادہ نشین کو دیا" (p. 304).
- **Shah Rahmat Ullah Qureshi**: at the urs "ملتانی دریا ہی [OCR?] بافناہی [OCR?] لاہور" give "فی گھر ایک سیر گندم اور ایک آنہ", more if anyone wishes (p. 329); something "ہر روز جمعرات"; on a fulfilled vow the votary brings "قند سیاہ"; "ہر روز میلہ کثرت [OCR?] سی" (p. 330).
- **Khoi Miran**: "اب تک اس کھوئی پر لوگ منت [OCR?] وغیرہ مانتے [OCR?] ہیں"; income to Mahtab Shah, who also receives something "فی [OCR?] شادی [OCR?]" in the village (p. 403).
- **Pir Roran-wala**: a pebble ("روڑا") taken from the grave, tied in a thread round the neck; on recovery a nadhr; the heap keeps growing; zamindars say "اکثر شہر [illegible] انکی نذر مقررہ ہے" (p. 437).
- **Ali Mardan Khan's alms**: food distributed to the poor at the mazar, flowers "دو سیر" laid there (p. 438).
- **Pir Mahka**: those "مہکون[OCR?] والہ" were cured by his prayer; still those who offer "جاروب و سہرہ گل" are cured, "چنانچہ اتبک[OCR?] یہ منتا[OCR?] بماری[OCR?] ہے" (p. 445).
- **Offerings at Lange**: "نذریں" still made at "موضع لنگے[OCR?]" (p. 449).
- **Unnamed saint of chunk 044**: the murids' descendants hold the yearly fatiha with sama and give nazr to the pirzada (p. 482).
- **Ajmer khanqah ijazat-namas** (p. 468): the saint tells the sajjada nashin in a dream to issue a petitioner an ijazat-nama for a sum, addressed to a named amir; sealed with "مہر خانقاہ"; the addressee pays at once; "اوسکو ہندو[OCR?] و مسلمان ساکنان ہندوستان سب مانتے[OCR?] ہیں".
- **Tank of Sayyid**: women bathe sick children there and distribute "شیرینی" or bread, especially for "خارش" and "سایہ زدہ" children (p. 604); in Abd al-Khaliq's lifetime the sick were told to bathe and were cured, and the custom continued (p. 605); a fixed day "یوم الثلثی[OCR?]" and "شہر بھر" coming (garbled) (p. 605); people still take earth from the ground to bathe sick children (p. 606).
- **Siraj-ud-Din Gilani**: "[illegible] کو یہاں چراغ ملتا ہے"; the residents of "پورہ اعوان[OCR?]" and the zamindars tell of his karamat (p. 624).
- **Jalali fire-dhamal** (p. 621): a goat or the like vowed as nazrana to the Chihil-tan; when granted, faqirs light coals in memory of "تنور حضرت سید جلال" and put out the fire with barefoot "دھمال", and only then is the nazr taken. Jalali faqirs in worldly work send their yearly surplus as nazrana to Hazrat Wadud[OCR?] Haqqani's mazar; no faqir keeps money (p. 623).
- **Shah Jamal**: double wages to labourers; a house given to a devotee; the house rent and four shops dedicated in his nazr (pp. 690–691); chilla-places still receive vows (p. 693).
- **Halim[OCR?] Shah / Shah Badr**: "جو چڑھاوہ ہوتا ہی فقیر کو دیتی ہین" (p. 671).
- **Karana seat**: "اہ بارک و مان ہزار ہا روپیہ چڑہ متی ہیں" (p. 550).
- **Mai Bhagi**: crowds of "لاکہوں لوگ"; nazr included the Maharaja's 500 rupees and the 10,000-rupee building (p. 790).
- **Shah Kamaita, Fazl Shah, Hazrat Ishan, Shah Bilawal**: see the urs subsection.

### The state and religious property: grants, demolition, confiscation, reuse, repair and courts

- **Successive states as patrons**: Mughal grants, the "ناظمان لاہور", Sikh rulers, and Ranjit Singh to Dalip Singh; land lost at the English settlement (pp. 88–89, 95).
- **Mughal and pre-Sikh**:
  - Shaikh Musa's tomb built by "اکبر[OCR?] بادشاہ کی والدہ" (p. 117); Shah Shams-ud-Din Qadiri's tomb built through Shah Jahan as prince, a garden around it until "عہد محمد شاہی [OCR?]" (p. 416); Mian Mir's rauza built by Dara Shikoh (pp. 289, 291), who planned a red-stone road to "قلعہ لاہور" (p. 291); Dara the stone parts and Alamgir the brick dome and the rest (p. 299).
  - Shah Rahmat Ullah Qureshi: masons were all engaged on "مسجد بادشاہی" and "کسیکو اجازت نہ تھی کہ کسی اور جگہ یہ کام کرے"; they agreed to build the tomb in one night, stopped before dawn "out of fear of the king", and "وہ گنبذ نا تیار رہ گیا" (p. 329).
  - Shaista Khan placed hafiz Quran-readers at Abu al-Hasan's tomb and made a waqf (p. 430).
  - Zeb-un-Nisa's land: it belonged to "دو سو[OCR?] زمیندار", who refused to sell, so she gave him land near Shahdara in exchange (p. 643).
  - Sa'dullah Khan: the merchant exempted from "محصول" "حسب وعدہ والدہ" (p. 634).
  - Sajjada dispute "بعہد شاہجہانی" at Abd al-Qadir Thani: Sayyid Muhammad Maruf, with access to Alamgir, claimed against Fath Muhammad, "میں یہی پوتا ہوں"; the case came before "حضرت شاہجہان بادشاہ غازی", who said he would come in person "بمزار معلی شیخ عبدالقادر ثانی" and give the "دستار سجادگی" (pp. 651–652; outcome under Legends).
  - Jahangir's tomb: "لاکہا روپیہ" spent; long neglected because of "تنزل سلطنت چغتائی و بادشاہ گردی" (p. 855); under Bahadur Shah, "حسب الحکم کسی ملا کی", a roof was built over the open-air marble grave-cover (p. 855).
  - Appointment of an "اتالیق[OCR?]" by the Nazim of Lahore (p. 14); a person apparently appointed under Jahangir to record Husain's words and inform [illegible], too damaged to state further (p. 59).
- **Sikh-period patronage**:
  - Basant at Madho Lal Husain (pp. 78–80): basanti dress for the whole court and army, tents at the mazar, troops lining the route from the fort (p. 78); 400–500 horses and two regiments[OCR?] of infantry; the Maharaja's cash offerings[OCR?]; the offering 1100[OCR?] rupees with a basanti shawl; robes of honour (p. 79); the nazr given out as a Basant prize to servants and troops; a month's pay to the troops with "موقوف[OCR?]", meaning unclear (p. 80).
  - Mauj Darya: "چالیس [OCR?] روپیہ ماہیانہ" plus nazr from the Maharaja (p. 105). Data Darbar: "سرکار سے بعہد مہاراجہ رنجیت سنگھ ایک ہزار[OCR?] روپیہ سالیانہ", with some sweets (p. 171). Bustan[OCR?] Shah, a majzub: an allowance from the Maharaja's treasury (p. 203).
  - Rasul Shahis: "امیرانِ حالیہ[OCR?] سکھاں" devoted to Nur Husain; "دو سو روپیہ[OCR?] ماہواری[OCR?]" for wine "بطور مدد ماہیانہ[OCR?]"; now under "سرکارِ گردوں وقار انگلشیہ[OCR?]" that support "کم آتا", and the faqirs "سوال بھی کسی سے کچھ نہیں کرتے" (p. 152).
  - Fazl Shah: "صدہا[OCR?] روپیہ کا حج[OCR?] ما ہواری[OCR?]" for his family, clothes and gold kept for them (p. 336); after the raja's death "سب کجہ[OCR?] منذ[OCR?] ہو گیا", though the raja's khalifa and relatives still give something (p. 337); his son Buland Shah has no fixed allowance, lives "گھر با رہ بیچ کر", the raja had given him "چار پانچ منزل" (p. 337).
  - Mian Wadda: "مہارامہ[OCR?] رحمیت[OCR?] سگہ[OCR?]" came and made a gift (p. 457). The Sikh place of p. 281 was greatly valued under Ranjit Singh (p. 281).
  - Under Diwan Kirpa Ram the urs and Thursday "مجری ناچ راگ" at Dori Shah were paid by the Diwan for his lifetime (p. 777).
  - Lehna Singh had a wooden roof put over Jahangir's grave; Kharak Singh repaired it; "اب تک وہ سقف چوبی [illegible] موجود ہے" (p. 855); the roof "جو بہادر شاہ نے توڑ کر بنائی تہی" is now blackened (p. 859).
  - Khoi Miran: "لہنا [OCR?] سنگھ [OCR?] حاکم" ordered the kardar to build a well with a wall; settlers told "تم کو [OCR?] دو سال محاصل [OCR?] معاف"; after two years settlement ("آبادی") began (p. 402).
  - Begumpura settled under Ranjit Singh: zamindars "مراد بخش اور خدا بخش" of Baghbanpura ordered to settle and cultivate it, Khuda Bakhsh made "نمبردار" (p. 721).
  - Maharaja Sher Singh gathered the jogis of Lahore and, after inquiry, ordered them to keep a Hindu Brahmin bread-maker and "ہندو[OCR?] لانگری[OCR?]", on pain of "دو مکان ضبط ہو کر … اور جاگیر[OCR?] بھی ضبط ہو جاویگی" (pp. 560–561).
- **Sikh-period demolition, seizure and stripping**:
  - Shaikh Ashraf's tomb and mosque outside the Bhati[OCR?] gate demolished by Ranjit Singh; he was reburied at the present place (p. 159).
  - Shah Muhammad Ghaus: Kharak Singh, "ولی عہد مہاراجہ [illegible]", ordered work concerning "رواجی[OCR?] شہر لاہور کی [illegible]" and the cutting of trees (p. 178); word came that the mazar would be demolished within two or three days (p. 179; outcome under Legends).
  - Mian Mir: public opinion that a king should not pull down former kings' buildings but repair them; the present "صاحبان عالیشاں دام اقبالہم و حشمتہم" repair old buildings so they remain as "یادگار" (p. 296).
  - Qutb al-Din's tomb demolished under Sikh rule, material sent to Amritsar; now a butcher-house and occupied by courtesans (p. 271).
  - Pir Hadi Rahnuma's stone pillars removed by "راجہ دیاں سنگ[OCR?]" (pp. 219–220); an enclosure wall tied to "ملازم راجہ[OCR?] دہیاں سنگہ[OCR?]" and "سمت[OCR?] ۱۸۹۹[OCR?]", builder and wall not legible (p. 190).
  - Mulla Shah's garden: "عمارت عالیشان ستونہائی سنگ سرخ تھی مگر بعہد سکہان[OCR?] مسمار ہوگئی" (p. 312).
  - Ali Mardan Khan's tomb: "یہ مقبرہ تمام سنگ سرخ و ابری[OCR?] کا تھا مگر سکھوں نے [illegible] سنگ ہا[OCR?] [illegible] … اٹھا لئے[OCR?]" (p. 413); its arched openings closed (see the Ranjit Singh bullet in the row) (p. 413).
  - Masjid Qassab Khana wala: Gulab Singh "[illegible] توڑا" the south wall (p. 424); damage by "گلاب سنگھ" throughout the Begumpura description (pp. 712–713, 719, 721); the Nawab's descendants "اپنی املاک سی بید خل[OCR?] رہی", yet "قبر و مسجد" survived, put down to the Nawab's "نیک کمائی" (p. 719).
  - Amir Khan's grave: "قبر امیر خان پر تعویذ[OCR?] بھی سنگ مرمر کا تھا مگر[OCR?] سکھ[OCR?] لوگ اتار کر لیگئے", now "قبر بخشتی[OCR?]" (p. 602).
  - "بعملداری سکھاں": Raja Suchet Singh "در سنمت[OCR?] ۱۸۹۱[OCR?] اٹھارہ سو نوے[OCR?]", "حسب الحکم مہاراج", turned Mian Khan's tomb into his own garden ("باغ خود آرا[OCR?]") (p. 627); marble from Parvez's tomb to the Darbar at Amritsar (p. 626); the tombstone torn from Mian Khan's grave (p. 629).
  - Zeb-un-Nisa's tomb: marble removed and floor stripped under Ranjit Singh (p. 637).
  - Marble from Jahangir's tomb to Amritsar (p. 855); Asif Khan's marble gate and mosque floor (pp. 861–862); Nur Jahan's tomb stripped (p. 863).
  - Confiscations: Rukn al-Din takia grant "ضبط" since the Sikh period (p. 348); Shah Chiragh's land lost "بعہد سکھان" before British rule (p. 109); Jiyo village "انعامداری سرکار خالصہ ضبط" (p. 613); Dori Shah's land taken over (p. 776); Lakhpat's houses "ضبط", partly given to "نواب [illegible] ملتانی[OCR?]" and partly sold, a descendant named only "[illegible] نامی" (p. 669); "کرایہ مکانات[OCR?]" in a damaged exchange with the Maharaja (p. 668).
  - Taksali-gate mosque held by a Hindu[OCR?] artillery officer since Ranjit Singh's time (pp. 754–755); Mai Lado mosque under Sikh rule ("باعث حکومت سکھا شاہی") occupied by a "سادہ مہد و", and when the city became desolate "حتیٰ کہ اسپر قبضہ ہندو کا ہو گیا" (pp. 226–227).
  - The siege of the khanqah (p. 460): the former sajjada nashin took "ایکر[OCR?] وبیہ[OCR?] نذر" to the raja and urged him to camp elsewhere; refused; Hira Singh's "تمام فوج سکھان معہ توب خانہ" besieged it; the officers warned the "مکان فقیر" would fall; Hira Singh promised "حبقدر[OCR?] گرسے[OCR?] گا ہم مرمت کرا دینگے"; Suchet[OCR?] Singh gave "صیصد[OCR?] روپیہ" to the "درویشان موجودہ خانقاہ" "لطور تصدق[OCR?]"; a wall fell under cannon fire; Suchet[OCR?] Singh was shot on a "چبوترہ" inside the "ڈیوڑی" and his head cut off.
  - Chowk by Wazir Khan's mosque "بعہد سکھاں" spoiled and shops built in it; now "حسب الحکم سہر کا[OCR?]" restored to its original form (p. 740).
  - Wazir Khan's baradari "بعہد سکھان" inside the cantonment ("داخل چھاؤنی") (p. 492). Jafar Khan's north wall "عہد مہاراجہ شیر سنگھ سے لے سبب بہ باران گر گئی ہے" (p. 495).
  - Sadr Diwan's graveyard under "عملداری[OCR?] سکھاں[OCR?]" reached "[illegible] ہزار گز[OCR?]" (p. 267). Shahid wala: corpses "سو سو دو دو سو یکجا کر کر ... گاڑ دیتے تھے"; "سکھوں" named; payments by "ٹھگوں[OCR?]" and "نذرانہ" (pp. 222–223).
- **British-period repair, release and grants**:
  - Anarkali tomb: "اب سرکار کی عملداری میں" mirrored panels fitted (p. 137). Gunbad Qasim Khan: "سفیدی دیواروں پر عہد انگریزی میں ہوئی[OCR?]" (p. 283). Wazir Khan's baradari: the sarkar fitted English frames ("چوکھٹ[OCR?] … انگریزی[OCR?]") (p. 491).
  - Bahawal Shah's takia: people had taken it in connection with the Ferozepur road; "مانٹگمری[OCR?] صاحب بہادر جو اس وقت کمشنر تہو[OCR?] واگذار کر دیا" and "منجانب سرکار[OCR?] تعمیر[OCR?] جاہ[OCR?] بہی[OCR?] کرا دی[OCR?]" (p. 365).
  - Mai Lado mosque: when "علمداری[OCR?] ... انگریزی[OCR?]" came, it was freed from the occupant and the brick enclosure built (p. 226).
  - The ex-commissioner of Lahore granted land for the Shivdwala (p. 121).
  - Hazrat Ishan's tomb: Khwaja Ahmad, brought from Kashmir by "سر ہنری [OCR?] لارنس [OCR?] صاحب بہادر", petitioned Lawrence at the khanqah for repair "سرکار کی طرف سی"; the sahib agreed, but Khwaja Ahmad died on the road to Hindustan and the repair was "ملتوی"; the mutawallis could not afford it (p. 382).
  - Jahangir's tomb: two notices, English and Persian, forbid any "صاحب انگریز یا کوئی رئیس" to enter with shoes; "سرکار ہماری کیسا خوب [OCR?] مراتب شاہان سلف کا رکہتی ہے" (p. 857); floor repaired with lime (p. 857); minarets under Wedderburn (p. 860); the west gate now a dak bungalow, "ایک روپیہ یومیہ", with a chaprasi, income to the government (p. 860); "تخمینا تیس [OCR?] ۳ [OCR?] ہزار روپیہ" spent since English rule (p. 863).
  - Kharak Singh's samadh: British-government expense and yearly repairs (p. 574).
  - Company Bagh built under English rule at about a lakh of rupees (p. 675).
  - Goldsmiths' graveyard "تا حال سرکار سے واگذار ہے[OCR?]" (p. 462). Naqiban mosque: "سرکار سی[OCR?] رسی[OCR?] واگذاری[OCR?]" (release from the sarkar?) (p. 331).
  - Shahid Ganj jagir released to Jiwan[OCR?] Singh with one-fourth nazrana (p. 769). Jawahar Singh's samadhi jagir confiscated in 1859[OCR?] and later restored (pp. 870–871).
- **British-period confiscation, purchase and clearance**:
  - Mohabbat Shah's land "اب ضبط سرکار ہے" (p. 491); a well's income "پچاس[OCR?] روپیہ سالانہ" enjoyed "چالیس[OCR?] برس" "اب ضبط ہو گئی", with "چاہ بلوچاں والہ" named alongside (p. 215).
  - Bahadur Khan tomb: the sarkar paid cash to empty it of Mehr Nura's[OCR?] heirs (p. 423). The government offered Rs 300 for the sirki-bands' takia to be vacated (p. 774).
  - Baradari and tank at Mian Mir: bricks dug up by "محمد سلطان ٹہیکہ دار" for "جہا ولی [OCR?] میا میر"; "دو سو بچاس [OCR?] روپیہ" paid to "محبوب شاہ سجادہ نشین حضرت میا میر" by "[illegible] صاحب بہادر [OCR?] یشی [OCR?] کمشنر لاہور" (p. 310).
  - Chauburji: "سرکار انگریزی نے [illegible] کاٹ ڈالا" (p. 342); land with "لارس [OCR?]" exchanged by "سرکار" into the "باغ سرکار"; a "بارہ دری" demolished "عملداری انگریزی" (p. 344).
  - Farid Astana: "حسب الحکم سرکار وہ ٹیلہ گرایا جانا [illegible]" (p. 228); much of the SW corner razed and old graves demolished, decayed bones visible (p. 229).
  - Burial orders: Rahim Bakhsh[OCR?] buried in "میانی" "بموجب حکم سرکار", not in the grave built for him at the takia (p. 371); "اب قبور جدیدہ[OCR?] یہاں ہونی[OCR?] بند ہیں" at Sadr Diwan (p. 267); government order closing new burials at the Abul Muali graveyard (p. 778); municipal control of the graveyard at the Shahid Ganj mound (p. 127).
- **Colonial reuse of religious buildings**:
  - The mukhannas tomb now an office with verandas next to the postmaster's office (p. 123); railway sahibs use a mosque near the Sarai (p. 122); an old mosque bought and a church built near the Shivdwala (p. 121); court papers ("کچہری کے لکھے") kept in seven[OCR?] doors of Abul Maali's tomb (p. 124).
  - Anarkali tomb: "صاحب فرانسیس[OCR?]" once lived there; now "گرجا گھر", a red-stone cross about two feet long on the central dome, curtains at the doors (p. 137).
  - Shah Chiragh: an adjoining mosque taken into an accounts office (pp. 108–109).
  - Pir Hadi Rahnuma: the south mosque now has a kothi in it and "کمپنی[OCR?] باغ" (pp. 219–220).
  - Qadam Rasul: "کلین[OCR?] صاحب متعینہ ریلوی" built a brick kothi in a corner of the inner enclosure (p. 333).
  - Bahadur Khan tomb used as a dance house by "صاحبان مالیتان[OCR?]", now with railway employees' sheds; all lower doors bricked up except one (p. 421).
  - Wazir Khan's baradari (p. 492): under "سرکار انگریزی" first a camp of "گورہ فوج"; when "چھاؤنی میاں میر" was set up, the "محکمہ بندوبست" was housed there; then the telegraph people ("صاحبان تار مخبر یعنی ٹیلیگراف والونکا"), then the "عجائب خانہ ضلع لاہور"; at the start of "سن چوہتر[OCR?]" used in connection with an "ایکزبیشن" (runs on to p. 493).
  - Dai Anga mosque now within railway premises (pp. 606–607); the Eidgah partly occupied by a "ملازم ریلوی[OCR?]", and "اس صاحب" closed the great door and fitted a door and two windows (pp. 708–709).
  - Tahli Sahib's tahli tree cut by the railway sahibs, now green again (p. 679); its land gone to the railway (p. 679).
  - The Dara-wife mosque sold to the railway (p. 772).
  - Mian Mir's maidan shrunk because of "سٹرک ریلوی ملتان [OCR?] و کوٹہی گین [OCR?] صاحب وغیرہ" (p. 305); the mela ground partly built over (p. 296). Chauburji: "اب وہاں ریل مرور [OCR?] ہے" (p. 344). By the Bangla runs "سڑک آہنی ملتان" (p. 497).
- **Courts, settlement and officials**:
  - A local tehsildar ordered a safety wall around the well after a death at the fair (p. 94).
  - Nizam Shah: after a Mughal ("قوم مغلی[OCR?]") man was shut in his "کاٹھہ[OCR?]", a complaint "بحکمہ[OCR?] صاحب ڈپٹی کمشنر بہادر ضلع لاہور", and the "کوتوال" sent to summon him (pp. 203–204).
  - Qutb Shah's takia: in the "بندوبست سابقہ" his name was entered as owner; now "امام بخش نمبردار" told the surveyors it was a mistake, "فی الاصل یہ مکان تکیہ شاملات دہ ہی"; Qutb Shah intends to petition the settlement officer (p. 239).
  - Sailani Shah burial dispute (p. 531): the numbardars claimed the land and opposed the burial; the body lay about "پانچ روز"; on their "استغاثہ نمبرداران" the tahsildar of Lahore came "بر سر موقع": "سیلانی شاہ کو یہاں ہی دفن کرو کہ ان فقیر و[OCR?] ٹکا[OCR?] قبضہ قدیمی ہے".
  - Khair Garh: a claim "با جلاس جناب مسٹر را برٹ[OCR?] ایلیس[OCR?] [illegible] صاحب بہادر" dismissed with leave to complain at the next settlement ("عہد ومست[OCR?] ثانی") (p. 447).
  - Mai Bhagi's takia: a claim by "سادات شمسی" "باجلاس مسٹر اجرٹن[OCR?] صاحب بہادر" dismissed (p. 791).
  - Muhammad Salih mosque: the imams declared before a court ("اقرار نامہ ... عدالت سی") that they claim nothing from the shop rents, which belong to the "مالی [OCR?]"; they get "ایک دو آنکو [OCR?]", the rest goes on rope and buckets (p. 754).
  - Numberdari dispute at Baghbanpura: before the settlement Nabi Bakhsh and Karim Bakhsh shared equally; Karim Bakhsh says Nabi Bakhsh became sole numberdar "مہربانی عملہ بندوبست سے", though by "حکم شرعی" they were equal sharers (p. 729).
  - "محکمہ بندوبست" appointed arbitrators in the chunk 079 offerings quarrel (p. 864).
  - Baradari: "نواب خان بہادر مرحوم"'s descendants through poverty cannot take possession or sue ("دیوانی مین نالش") (p. 443).
  - Fazl Shah's takia: a faqirs' kotha bought for "سترہ رویہ" and "مکان لشکر گرا دیا"; the faqirs: "ہمارا امت[OCR?] بقتماں[OCR?] ہوا ہم عرصی[OCR?] دین گے" (p. 340).
  - Shah Gada bhandara: a suit "سرکار میں نالش" considered and dropped (p. 700).
  - Babu Baj Singh[OCR?] section: zamindars complained to the "سرکار [OCR?]" that "پیر کبیر [OCR?] کے روضہ کو خرابت [OCR?] کرنا ہمارا [illegible] ہمت نہیں [OCR?]"; after that something "کا موقوف [OCR?] ہوا" (p. 420).
  - Jogi court suit dismissed (pp. 564–565); jogis avoid courts (p. 562).

### Land disputes, encroachment and loss (non-state)

- Shah Muhammad Ghaus: people "دبا بیٹھے[OCR?] ہیں" part of the jagir (p. 178).
- Graveyard land cultivated: "گور کن لوگ یہاں کہتے ہیں کہ سردار خان مثنی[OCR?] ... زمین کو ... کاشت کرا لی ہے ۔ مگر فی الاصل یہ زمین متعلقہ گورستان مہمانی[OCR?] ہے" (p. 193).
- Zanjani khanqah: the sajjada nashin complains the khanqah's land has passed into Lala Ratan Chand's [illegible] and a tank made on it; buildings now hide it ("خانقاہ بالکل نظر نہیں آتی") (p. 271).
- Takia Mutahhar Shah's faqir complains land was seized by a municipal committee member (p. 521). Takia Tamarind: owned by Sayyid Muhammad Shah Gilani's line but held by Sayyid Nizam al-Din Gilani; Muhammad Shah: "مکان تو … ہمارے بزرگان کا ہے … ہم تو کہتے ہیں کہ وہی مالک بن جاویں" (p. 523).
- Amir Khan's mosque: "اس مسجد میں قبضہ زمینداران موضع گدہ[OCR?] بھی [illegible] ہے"; a field "حال مقبوضہ قایم[OCR?] پسر غلام نبی" (p. 602).
- Shahid Ganj vs the Sahib Singh samadh: Ganda Singh "ہاتہہ ڈالتا ہی" on it, claiming Sahib Singh was a chela of his father Jiwan Singh; no one confirms it, all say "گنڈا سنگ ناحق دست اندازی کرتا ہی" (p. 771).
- Samadh enclosure under cultivation: "چار دیواری میں اب دم تحریر زراعت[OCR?] ہوتی ہے", held by "سجان سنگھ[OCR?] و لہنا سنگھ[OCR?]" (p. 665).
- Lakhpat's tank near "موضع مزنگ[OCR?]": land bought by "زمینداران مزنگ[OCR?]", "اب اس میں زراعت ہوتی ہے" (p. 669); "دہرم سنگھ[OCR?] نانک پنتھی[OCR?]" took the baradari about "بیس[OCR?] برس" ago, repaired it and set a well running (pp. 669–670); "سرکار انگریزی" coming to his house (damaged) (p. 670).
- Jani Khan's tomb: the old garden's deorhi bought "از عرصہ دو سال" "بقیمت ایک سو ایک روپیہ" by "شیر از سینہ دار[OCR?]"; a "میندار[OCR?]" has put "تورٹھی[OCR?]" inside (p. 623).
- Mian Khan's tomb-garden (pp. 627–630): "ابتدائی علمداری انگریزی میں یہ باغ شیخ امام الدین صاحب کو ملا تھا", who tore up the black stone floor, now gone "قریب بضعف[OCR?]" (p. 629); "شیخ امام الدین صاحب مختار رہی اب نواب علی رضا خان صاحب مالک ہیں" (p. 629); the Nawab acquired it ("قیمت دو ہزار ڈھائی سو") and "مرمت شروع کی ہے" (p. 630); also called the garden of "نواب علی خان صاحب قزلباش[OCR?]" (p. 627).
- Brick theft: "حست [OCR?] فروتنان [OCR?] کشمیر [OCR?]" carried [bricks] off Hazrat Ishan's tomb, read as "خشت فروشان"; compare p. 379 (p. 382); Sardar Khan Bhat's platform bricks taken by "خشت فروشوں" (pp. 429–430); Kashmiri brick-sellers found Jawahar Singh's tahkhana (p. 493).
- Ali Mardan Khan's garden: a cistern demolished "معرفت محمد سلطان" at the request of "زمینداراں[OCR?]" (p. 412); "چند اشخاص سکونت پذیر ہیں", "کرایہ کوئی نہیں لیتا" (p. 413).

### Urs, mela, Basant and other observances

- **Madho Lal Husain / Hussain khanqah**: "الی الان[OCR?] بروز بسنت انکی مزار پر سرود و سماع و رقص و رنگ اندازی ہوتی ہی" (p. 58), attached to **madho-lal-hussain**; origin in Husain's yearly keeping of Basant after Madho coloured him (p. 58). Fair crowds "ساٹھ[OCR?] ستر[OCR?] ہزار آدمی", riders and camels separately, district hakims present (p. 77); "دن رات میں ہزار ہا روپیہ کی شراب فروخت ہو جاتی ہے", fine clothes (p. 77). British-period Basant prizes by order of the hakims: a prize for "آسمان[OCR?]" (damaged), a prize for an Arab-bred mare "بہ رنگ سبزہ[OCR?]", "قیمت مبلغ تیرہ سو[OCR?]"; a "کمیٹی[OCR?]" credited (p. 80). The fair calendar reset by solar date to 14 Baisakh (p. 89). Husain "نغمہ و رقص کنان[OCR?]" (p. 62), "رقص کنان" (pp. 63, 65); he taught tafsir in madrasas (p. 54) and led tarawih (p. 50).
- **Hindu observances**: Basant Panchami[OCR?] "حسب رسم و معمول" with dancing, near Holi (p. 58); bathing in the Ganga "غسل کا ثواب[OCR?] عظیم" (p. 59).
- **Mauj Darya**: urs once a year "بتاریخ ۱۷ [OCR?] ربیع الاول": "رات کو چراغاں", in the morning "مجلس ناچ طوائفان و سرود قوالان"; spectators stay the night (p. 105).
- **Shah Chiragh**: urs in "ربیع الثانی [OCR?]" (p. 108); a weekly Thursday "خوب [illegible] بجتی ہے" (p. 109).
- **Abdullah Shah Qadri**: daily mela "[illegible] الاول[OCR?] کی آٹھویں سے گیارہویں[OCR?] تک"; "تیسرے روز بلوچ لوگ ساکنان مزنگ[OCR?]" hold his urs (p. 111).
- **Shah Abu Ishaq**: in Muharram from the fifth, yearly; Quran reciters; fatiha over meat; "قوالی راگ [illegible] کی یہاں بالکل ممانعت ہے اور کبھی وقوع میں نہیں آئی" (p. 116).
- **Shah Abul Maali**: "یہاں سال ہر میں ایک دفعہ ۷[OCR?] ربیع الاول کو جو تاریخ وفات حضرت کی ہی میلہ ہوتا"; melas at both Eids (p. 126).
- **Shahid Ganj samadh**: Granth reading, heavy use of bhang, a former "فیض عام" under "مہاراجہ صاحب" (p. 123).
- **Unnamed shrine of chunk 011**: urs "بتاریخ سولہویں ربیع الاول"; each family distributes "نان گوشت پلاؤ حلوا" at home (p. 133); "بتاریخ ۱۵[OCR?] ـ ربیع الاول" the mazar washed ("مزار کو غسل دیکر"), urs the next day with night vigil and khatm (pp. 133–134).
- **Taj Shah**: urs on the death-date "روز دو شنبہ ہفتم ماہ بسیا[OCR?] کہ سمت ۱۹۰۱[OCR?] مطابق سنہ ۱۲۶۱[OCR?] بارہ سو سات[OCR?] ہجری"; many stay the night, "رنگ[OCR?] ناچ بھی ہوتا ہی" (p. 135); money for the lamp ("چراغ") (p. 134).
- **Shah Kalu Chishti**: "طوائفاں[OCR?] و سماع قوالوں", chiraghan, halwa (p. 144).
- **Haji Muhammad Saeed**: "اس تاریخ کو میلا بھی ہوتا ہی"; his followers say formerly there was no mela (p. 159). Revered from Kabul: "قبائل ... محمد خان والی کابل" and relatives his murids; "سردار سلطان محمد خان بہادر خان[OCR?] مرحوم" offered "نذر و نیاز" at the mazar when in Lahore (p. 159).
- **Data Darbar**: on the urs day food prepared for faqirs in the kitchen-store ("با ورچی خانہ و گنجینہ"), "دو تنور" (pp. 165–166); "تقسیم نان و نعم شرایف[OCR?]" (p. 167); "حلوا و دال سنخو[OCR?] معہ گوشت", khadims distribute "نذریں" (p. 171).
- **Shah Muhammad Ghaus**: urs "تاریخ ۱۷ [OCR?] ربیع الاول"; visitors "اکثر عورات و مرد" with offerings ("[illegible] لیکر"); time since death "ایکسو[OCR?] چار[OCR?] سال" (p. 178).
- **Shah Bilawal**: urs with "لاہور کے کتنے [OCR?] قبیل [OCR?] لوگ", "آتشباری [OCR?]", "چکہ چلانے [OCR?]", called "رسم" (p. 186).
- **Nizam Shah**: thousands of Hindus and Muslims at his funeral; a "میلہ" (p. 204).
- **Shaikh Tahir's urs**: dal and meat; ulama from "مزنگ ورلاہور[OCR?]" recite the khatm; large crowd; Quran all night; formerly only fatiha and water; with the new sajjada nashin a "مجلس قوالاں" began (p. 216).
- **Shah Kaku**: mela in Rabi al-Awwal by "خوجہ لوگ", all-night dancing, chiraghan, carpets, sa'iban and qanat (p. 219).
- **Shah Sharaf**: melas "۱۴[OCR?] رجب اور ۱۳[OCR?] صفر" (pp. 221–222); free teaching "فی سبیل اللہ" in the mosque (p. 222).
- **Syed Ismail**: about "چالیس[OCR?]" graves; urs on "۸[OCR?] رجب", "جوری[OCR?] شیریں" distributed; few people (p. 223).
- **Mai Lado mosque**: urs twice a year, in "ربیع [margin: الاولی]" and "رجب", held by Mehr Shah, "باج راگ رنگ", distribution to faqirs (p. 226); Mai Lado's dars and Friday sermons (p. 227).
- **Pakpattan**: crowds at the door said to give safety, "لاکہہ در لاکہہ آدمی" (p. 234). **Farid Astana**: yearly mela "پنجم محرم"; barbers; murids shave their heads and eat "کہل گوٹر", sold at stalls; black thread ("پٹرا[OCR?]" in Panjabi) sold and worn as nazr; on Ashura, Shab-e Barat and every Thursday flowers and salam (p. 235).
- **Qutb Shah's Sawan fair**: for some years in Sawan he cooked "کہہیر[OCR?] یعنی شیر برنج" and held a mela; the city's Gujars gave that day's milk; now dropped (p. 239); a fatiha in "حب[OCR?]" (damaged) (p. 239).
- **Bhagat's site**: great crowds, people stay "شنبہ[OCR?]" dawn to evening, two days of sama', qawwals paid (p. 244).
- **Sakhi Sarwar mela in Lahore**: "ماہ بہاگن[OCR?] کے نوخندہ[OCR?] منگل" (p. 255); "تمام مخلوقات اکثر ارزال اور عورات فا[حشہ]" (p. 255); hundreds from "لاہوری دروازہ" to "انارکلی", hundreds of Bharai[OCR?] dhol players (p. 255); "بہمنیاں[OCR?]": Bharai[OCR?] take boys in their laps and dance, families give something [illegible] (pp. 255–256). Sakhi Sarwar's sang halts at the Jahangir sarai (p. 861).
- **Zanjani khanqah**: urs "سومہویں [OCR?] رجب المرجب"; "پنڈھارہ [OCR?] کل فقرا کا تقسیم ہوتا", nan, gosht, pulao and qaliya; next day "مجلسِ صوفیہ با قوالی ہوتی ہے"; now "صرف [OCR?] جمعرات کے دن چند زن و مرد" (p. 271). Shah Hussain Zanjani: urs once a year (p. 705).
- **Imambara**: monthly stipend from Nawab Ali Raza Khan; the Zuljanah procession in Muharram (p. 273); the Sunni–Imamiyya riot on Ashura is under Other saints (pp. 272–273).
- **Shah Shutrbani**: melas "یازدہم صفر المظفر" (wisal) and "دہم [OCR?] ربیع الاول"; Safar fatiha with the mosque's mullas; Rabi al-Awwal "نان [OCR?] گوشت"; "تمام گدی نشیں و فقرا جمع ہوتی ہیں" (p. 276).
- **Goldsmiths' pir-khana**: urs "ماہ[OCR?] بیساکھ[OCR?]", the goldsmiths' mela (p. 279). **Goldsmiths' graveyard**: gather "ہر ماہ کی دو جمعہ" and arrange repairs then (p. 463).
- **Takia Sikandar Shah**: "بروز رویت ہلال عید تمام چرس خوار لوگ جمع ہوکر سوا روپیہ[OCR?] کی چلم" (p. 283).
- **Mian Mir urs and mela**: "ہفتم ربیع الاول"; shops from gate to chabutra, "صد ہا" halwai and fruit stalls, a "می فروشی[OCR?]" stall, falooda makers and toy sellers (p. 295); "اکھاڑی[OCR?]" of charas-smokers and water carriers; hundreds of carts, ekkas, camels, dolis and ponies (p. 296); visitors sit on the chabutra at the mela (p. 295); the north gate sometimes opened on the urs (p. 295); the mela in decline as houses were built on its ground, "یہ میلہ [illegible] تمام ہونی لگا" (p. 296). "تمام رات راگ ناچ رنگ"; courtesans perform "مجرا جوگی" without fee; the devout worship while "ہزار ہا عیاش" waste money and drink ("می نوشی [OCR?]"); "اب بباعث اجرای ریل و قریب چہاونی [OCR?] میا میر" huge crowds; next day qawwali morning to evening, food cooked in many places (p. 305). "سیر عرس بہی آٹہہ یوم [OCR?]" (garbled) (p. 304). Rainy-season melas in Sawan and "بہادہون [OCR?]" every Wednesday night ("ہر ایک بدہ کی شب") for two months, thousands attending, "اوس رات دروازہ شہر بہی کہلا رہتا ہی" (p. 305). The urs as the public turban ceremony (p. 319).
- **Shah Rahmat Ullah Qureshi**: urs "تاریخ ۷ [OCR?] صفر المظفر" (p. 329).
- **Qadam Rasul**: "گیارہویں ہمیشہ ربیع الاول کی ماہ وفات کہ[OCR?] شاہ رسالت ہی یہان عرس ہوتا ہے", "بھنڈارہ[OCR?] فقرا[OCR?] تقسیم[OCR?]" (p. 333).
- **Fazl Shah**: "میلا معہ محلس[OCR?] انکا سال سال ماہ ساون کی اکیسویں ہوتا ہی"; something with "گوشت" (garbled); Kashmiri people in connection with the urs (garbled) (p. 336); "ہمیشہ بیان قوال آتی مین … کبھی کبھی مجرا یعنی رقص طوایفان بھی ہو جاتا ہی" (p. 339).
- **Ghori Shah takia**: townspeople come to smoke "حقہ تماکو"; bhang ("بنگ") kept (p. 339).
- **Rukn al-Din takia**: urs of "ساہ گو شاہ [OCR?]" on "بائیسویں [OCR?] شعبان", of Hazrat Muhammad Siddiq "۴ [OCR?] م [OCR?] ذوالحج", of Muhammad Salim "تیسری ذوالحج"; "ہر روز چراغ جلتا ہی" (pp. 347–348).
- **Shah Kamaita**: "بروز عرس بہنڈ ہارہ [OCR?] رات کو ہوتا ہی و دوسری روز ناچ گنجریان [OCR?] اور مجلس ہوتی ہی"; later no big fair ("اسپلا [OCR?]") but some come "راگ رنگ" (p. 349).
- **Bahawal Shah's takia**: mela in "صفر"; lamps every "جہرا[OCR?]" night; the faqir lives by "گدائی[OCR?]" (p. 366).
- **Hazrat Ishan's fatiha** (p. 379): "نواب خان بھا [OCR?]" "نہایت معتقد [OCR?]"; on the "آٹھوں [OCR?] دن" "تمام فقرا [OCR?] [illegible] لاہور کے" gathered; each got "دو دو نان اور آدھ [OCR?] سیر [OCR?] حلوا [OCR?]" and "ایک ایک روپیہ نقد"; "ختم خواجگان نقشبندیہ [OCR?]" recited; a "تسبیح خانہ [OCR?]"; "معبد سکھان [OCR?] سے [OCR?]" begins a damaged line.
- **Hamid Qari**: "بتاریخ ١٨[OCR?] جمادی الثانی ہر سال عرس آپ کا ہوتا ہی رہے[OCR?]" (p. 411).
- **Shah Shams-ud-Din Qadiri**: the khadim says "حضرت کی مرضی نہیں کہ کوئی یہاں رات کو شب باش [OCR?] ہوے [OCR?]" because "ہیبت [OCR?] آتی ہی"; a lamp lit in the morning; fatiha "ہر دو وقت [OCR?]"; "کوئی عرس کلاں یہاں نہیں ہوتا" (p. 416).
- **Mian Wadda**: "اب میاں کوی عرس وغیرہ و تو نہیں ہوتا", but his descendants hold an annual fatiha in their village (p. 428).
- **Ghore Shah**: "۱۲[OCR?] رجب کو میلہ ہوتا ہے", something on "۲۳[OCR?] [illegible]", "موضع [illegible]" (p. 433); a gathering "ماہ رمضان کی [illegible: numerals] کو" at Miran Sayyid Wali's mazar (p. 434); urs on his death date with faqirs and qawwali (p. 434).
- **Bhage Shah**: a yearly mela at Namani Shah's maqbara near Garhi Sanaho[OCR?]; "صدہا چیلے اسکے حلالخور[OCR?] وغیرہ"; qawwali "حسب دستور نوشاہیان" (p. 466).
- **Naushahi urs**: damaged references to an urs in "ربیع الاول" (p. 485); Sachiyar's urs at Naushehra with pomp (p. 485); Pak Rahman's urs at "موضع نڑی[OCR?]" (p. 485); an urs with crowds, "قوالی" and a "روز میلہ" (p. 485).
- **Mohabbat Shah's takia**: fatiha on the eleventh ("یا رہو میں فانخہ[OCR?]") with "نان گوشت" (p. 490).
- **Takia Kamil Shah**: an "عرس" in a garbled line (p. 530).
- **Shah Madar**: urs yearly in "ماہ جمادی الاول" at the rauza; Dara Shikoh in "سفینۃ الاولیاء": "چھ[OCR?] لاکھ آدمی" gather from all Hindustan (p. 538).
- **Sayyid Mahmud**: "قدیم الایام سے تاریخ ۱۱[OCR?] ربیع الثانی عرس حضرت سید محمود صاحب کا [illegible] سے ہوتا ہے"; a few gather, "خصوصاً لوہار[OCR?] اور [illegible] فروش", stay the night with qawwali; later "ناچ راگ رنگ" began; now "ہر روز مقرر ہی عرس ہوتا ہے", faqirs cook "نان گوشت" (p. 596); the descendants hold the "ہر روز عرس علیحدہ" (p. 599).
- **Fazil Shah's takia**: a faqir held an urs with a "حلقہ فقرا"; the place became "بالکل خراب و ویران", now in a named (illegible) person's care, fatiha held (p. 603).
- **Hasu Teli**: urs "تیسری شوال کو مقرر"; "تمام تیلی قوم" attends and stays the night; sweet dishes, "راگ و رنگ" all night; next day "نان و گوشت" and "نوالی[OCR?]" (p. 608); fatiha on the death day (p. 610).
- **Haji Abd al-Karim**: urs "بیست و ہفتم[OCR?] ماہ رجب یا ماہ رجب بشب معراج شریف" (p. 639).
- **Ali Rangrez**: urs once a year; the dyers of Lahore attached to the khanqah (pp. 659–660).
- **Halim[OCR?] Shah / Shah Badr**: urs "تاریخ ۱۲[OCR?] ماہ ربیع الاول"; "بروز عرس دال گوشت نان بطور بھنڈارہ تقسیم کرتی ہین" (p. 671).
- **Pir Makki and Shah Dami Shah**: Pir Makki's urs and mela under that row (p. 675); Shah Dami Shah's urs and Thursday gatherings (pp. 675–676).
- **Shah Jamal**: urs and repairs funded from an assigned source (p. 692).
- **Fata Shah[OCR?]**: urs "ہفتم شوال" (stated for the Gobindwal place), "روٹی گوشت با دال[OCR?]"; Thursday attendance and chadhat in a damaged line (p. 697).
- **Shah Gada**: now at the urs a few faqirs gather and give fatiha (p. 700); bhandara dispute under the faqir subsection (pp. 699–700).
- **Marg Naini takia**: a yearly "مسلامی[OCR?]" (mela?) with offerings in rupees (amount illegible) and a "مجلس قوالاں" (p. 710).
- **Imam Gamun**: "اب تک سال بسال عرس انکا معہ مجلس ہوتا ہی" (p. 747).
